Grammar & commentary

Uttama Carita · उत्तमचरितम् · Chapter 8 of 13

रक्तबीजवधःThe Slaying of Raktabīja

raktabījavadhaḥ

63 mantras · 8.1–8.63

❁

Overview

Medhas takes up the story (8.1). With Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa slain and their many troops destroyed (8.2), Śumbha, his mind mastered by anger, orders the whole Daitya host to march (8.3): eighty-six clans with weapons raised and eighty-four clans of the Kambus (8.4), fifty Koṭivīrya clans and a hundred of the Dhaumras (8.5), the Kālakas, Daurhṛdas, Mauryas and Kālakeyas (8.6); and for the first time he sets out himself, with many thousands of troops (8.7). Seeing him come, Caṇḍikā fills the space between earth and sky with the twang of her bowstring (8.8); the lion roars, and Ambikā swells the roar with her bell (8.9); Kālī, her mouth gaping, outdoes all three sounds with her own (8.10). The Daitya armies close in on the Goddess, the lion and Kālī (8.11). At that moment, to destroy the gods’ enemies, the Śaktis of Brahmā, Śiva, Skanda, Viṣṇu and Indra leave their gods’ bodies and come to Caṇḍikā, each with her god’s form, ornaments and mount (8.12–14): Brahmāṇī in a swan-drawn car with rosary and water-pot (8.15), Māheśvarī on the bull with trident, serpent bracelets and crescent moon (8.16), Kaumārī with spear and peacock (8.17), Vaiṣṇavī on Garuḍa with conch, discus, mace, bow and sword (8.18), Vārāhī in the body of the Sacrificial Boar (8.19), Nārasiṃhī, whose tossing mane scatters the stars (8.20), and Aindrī, thunderbolt in hand, on the king of elephants, thousand-eyed (8.21). Śiva, surrounded by them, asks Caṇḍikā to slay the asuras at once (8.22). From the Goddess’s own body comes forth her Śakti, howling like a hundred jackals (8.23), and sends Śiva himself as her messenger to Śumbha and Niśumbha (8.24–25): let Indra have the three worlds and the gods their offerings, and let the asuras go to Pātāla if they wish to live — or come and feed her jackals (8.26–27). So she is known as Śivadūtī (8.28). The asuras march in indignation to where Kātyāyanī stands (8.29) and rain weapons on her (8.30); she cuts them down as if in play (8.31). Kālī ranges at the front with spear and skull-staff (8.32); Brahmāṇī saps the enemy with water from her pot (8.33); Māheśvarī, Vaiṣṇavī and Kaumārī strike with trident, discus and spear (8.34); Aindrī’s thunderbolt fells them in hundreds (8.35); Vārāhī gores them with snout and tusks (8.36); Nārasiṃhī tears them with her claws and devours them (8.37); Śivadūtī fells them with her laughter and eats them (8.38). The asura soldiers slip away (8.39), and Raktabīja advances (8.40): whenever a drop of his blood falls to the ground, an asura of his own stature springs up (8.41). Aindrī’s thunderbolt draws his blood (8.42), and warriors with his form and strength rise from every drop and fight the Mothers (8.43–45); a second stroke yields thousands (8.46); Vaiṣṇavī’s discus and Aindrī’s mace (8.47) fill the world with his doubles (8.48); Kaumārī, Vārāhī and Māheśvarī strike him (8.49), he strikes back at each Mother with his mace (8.50), and from every wound hundreds more arise (8.51), until the whole world is overrun and the gods are seized with fear (8.52). Caṇḍikā tells Kālī — Cāmuṇḍā — to open her mouth wide (8.53), to catch the blood her weapons draw and the asuras born of it (8.54), and to range the field devouring them, so that, drained of blood, he will perish (8.55–56); then she strikes him with her spear (8.56). Kālī takes his blood in her mouth; his mace-blow on Caṇḍikā (8.57) causes her not the slightest pain, while his blood pours out (8.58). Cāmuṇḍā catches it wherever it flows, eats the asuras born in her mouth and drinks his blood (8.59–60), and the Goddess strikes him down with spear, thunderbolt, arrows, swords and blades; he falls, bloodless (8.60–62). The gods rejoice beyond measure, and the band of Mothers born from them dances, wild with blood (8.62–63).

  • The Mothers: the gods’ powers in the gods’ forms
  • Śivadūtī: the Goddess who sends Śiva
  • Raktabīja: an enemy multiplied by every wound
  • Cāmuṇḍā’s mouth: ending the source, not the copies
  • Battle in sound before battle in arms
About this chapter
Forms of the Devī
Caṇḍikā (चण्डिका, 8.8, 8.13, 8.22, 8.53, 8.57), the Goddess at the centre of the battle, also Ambikā (8.9), the Devī (8.11, 8.30, 8.56, 8.60), Kātyāyanī (8.29) and ‘the Unconquered’ (अपराजिता, 8.24); Kālī (8.10, 8.11, 8.32, 8.53, 8.57), called by her name of chapter 7, Cāmuṇḍā (8.53, 8.59, 8.60, 8.61), the mouth that drinks Raktabīja’s blood; the Śaktis of the gods, each in her god’s likeness (8.13–14): Brahmāṇī (8.15, 8.33), Māheśvarī (8.16, 8.34, 8.49), Kaumārī — called अम्बिका गुहरूपिणी, ‘the Mother in the form of Guha’ (8.17) — Vaiṣṇavī (8.18, 8.34, 8.47), Vārāhī (8.19, 8.36, 8.49), Nārasiṃhī (8.20, 8.37) and Aindrī (8.21, 8.35, 8.42, 8.47), together ‘the band of Mothers’ (मातृगण, 8.39, 8.40, 8.63); and Caṇḍikā’s own Śakti, howling like a hundred jackals, named Śivadūtī because she made Śiva her messenger (8.23, 8.28, 8.38). Bhavānī, of the dhyāna, is the form of the liturgy, not of the text.
Significance of the events
This is the third and greatest of the forces sent against the Devī after Dhūmralocana (chapter 6) and Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa (chapter 7): not a general and his army but the whole Daitya host, with Śumbha himself at its head (8.3–7), though he does not meet her until chapter 9. The chapter brings two new things into the war. First, the Mothers: the powers of the gods leave their bodies and fight for Caṇḍikā in the gods’ own likeness (8.13–14). The text here calls them the gods’ śaktis; only later does the Goddess tell Śumbha that all these powers are hers and take them back into herself (10.5–6). Second, an enemy whom wounding only multiplies (8.41): every Mother’s blow fills the world with more Raktabījas (8.43–52), and the battle the Mothers are winning becomes the gods’ fear. The turn is not a stronger weapon but Cāmuṇḍā’s mouth (8.53–61), the devouring power born in chapter 7 (7.9–13), which catches the blood before it reaches the ground. Between the two stands Śivadūtī, the Goddess’s own Śakti, who makes Śiva himself her envoy (8.24–28) and offers the asuras peace on the gods’ terms (8.26–27).
Sanskrit patterns to watch
A narrative chapter of perfects — reduplicated (आदिदेश, निर्जगाम, ययुः, आययौ, जघान, चिच्छेद, चचार, चखाद, सुस्राव, समुत्तस्थुः, ववाह, जगृहे, पपौ, पपात, ननर्त), the unreduplicated पेतुः and नेशुः (8.35, 8.39), the ātmanepada युयुधे beside the epic युयुधुः (8.42, 8.45), and the periphrastic पूरयामास, ताडयामास (8.8, 8.47) — beside imperfects (व्यचरत्, अकरोत्, न्यपतन्, अताडयत्), one without its augment (उपबृंहयत्, 8.9) and one epic thematic form (अहनत्, 8.50). Past participles stand for finite verbs at each arrival (आयाता, प्राप्ता, विनिष्क्रान्ता, 8.15–23). Commands come in three persons: Śumbha’s third-person imperatives to absent troops (निर्यान्तु, निर्गच्छन्तु, 8.4–6), Śiva’s passive imperative (हन्यन्ताम्, 8.22), and the Goddess’s second-person orders (गच्छ, ब्रूहि, 8.24–25; आगच्छत, 8.27; कुरु, प्रतीच्छ, चर, 8.53–55). The chapter is built on correlatives: locative absolutes (8.2), यस्य … यद्रूपम् … तद्वत् (8.14), यतः … ततः (8.28), येन येन … स्म (8.33), यदा … तदा (8.41), यावन्तः … तावन्तः (8.44). The Mothers’ names are vṛddhi derivatives with the feminine -ī (माहेश्वरी, कौमारी, वैष्णवी, वाराही, नारसिंही, ऐन्द्री) or -ānī (ब्रह्माणी). Sentences run across verses at 8.2–3, 8.12–13, 8.59–60, 8.60–61 and 8.61–62.
The teaching, as distinct from the narrative
Literally, the gods’ powers come out of their bodies to fight beside the Goddess, an asura whose every drop of blood becomes another asura fills the world with his doubles, and Kālī drinks his blood so that he can be killed. As teaching, the chapter shows the one power acting in many forms: each Mother is a god’s power in that god’s shape (8.14), yet Kaumārī is called simply ‘the Mother’ (8.17), and the tradition, following 10.5–6, reads all of them as the Devī’s own. In the Śākta reading Śiva’s errand (8.24–28) shows the god acting at the bidding of Śakti; that is a reading of the tradition, not a statement of the verse, which says only that she sent him and so won her name. Raktabīja is often read today as a picture of desire or habit that grows every time it is fought on its own terms; that reading is modern. What the verses themselves say is plain: striking him harder only multiplied him (8.43–52), and he died when his blood was received before it touched the ground (8.54–61).
Carita
Uttama Carita (the third episode, chapters 5–13; the Gita Press viniyoga at the head of chapter 5 calls it the uttara-caritra). In the recitation tradition its presiding form is Mahāsarasvatī; this is the Gita Press viniyoga, not a statement of the Purāṇa text. Chapter 8 has no viniyoga of its own in the pāṭha, only a dhyāna: ‘I contemplate Bhavānī, red, her eyes rippling with compassion, her hands holding the noose, the goad, the arrow and the bow, surrounded by rays — the powers beginning with aṇimā — as “I” myself’ (अरुणां करुणातरङ्गिताक्षीं … अहमित्येव विभावये भवानीम्). The same verse, with पुष्पबाणचापाम् ‘the flower-arrow and the bow’ in place of बाणचापहस्ताम्, is the second dhyāna verse of the Lalitā Sahasranāma. The meditation belongs to the liturgy; the chapter itself does not mention it.
Recited before the chapter

These lines belong to the recitation (the Gita Press pāṭha), not to the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa text, and are kept apart from the numbered mantras.

Dhyāna

ॐ अरुणां करुणातरङ्गिताक्षींधृतपाशाङ्कुशबाणचापहस्ताम्।अणिमादिभिरावृतां मयूखै-रहमित्येव विभावये भवानीम्॥

ॐ aruṇāṃ karuṇātaraṅgitākṣīṃdhṛtapāśāṅkuśabāṇacāpahastām |aṇimādibhirāvṛtāṃ mayūkhai-rahamityeva vibhāvaye bhavānīm ||

Verse 8.1

Speaker
‹›

ॐऋषिरुवाच॥⁠१⁠॥

ṛṣiruvāca ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ऋषिः · उवाच

ṛṣiḥ · uvāca

Word by word

  1. ऋषिःṛṣiḥthe Ṛṣi, the seer (Medhas)m. nom. sg. of ऋषि (i-stem); subject of उवाच
  2. उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, kartari prayoga

Translation

The Ṛṣi said:

In readable EnglishThe Seer said:

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    The final s of ऋषिस् becomes ru. Not being at a pause or before an unvoiced sound, it does not become visarga (खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः, 8.3.15, does not apply) and surfaces as r before the vowel: ऋषिरुवाच. After i (not a/ā) the r stays.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • उवाच uvāca

    Perfect of √वच् with samprasāraṇa in the reduplicated syllable (व → उ): उ-वाच. The standing formula of the speaker labels, as in 1.1.

Anvaya · prose order

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Medhas, the seer of the frame story, takes up the narrative again for King Suratha (whom he will address as नृप and भूप in this chapter). Chapter 7 ended with Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa slain and Kālī named Cāmuṇḍā (7.27); chapter 8 tells of the Mothers and Raktabīja. In recitation this mantra carries the bīja ॐ, which belongs to the pāṭha, not to the verse.

Verse 8.2

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

चण्डे च निहते दैत्ये मुण्डे च विनिपातिते।बहुलेषु च सैन्येषु क्षयितेष्वसुरेश्वरः॥⁠२⁠॥

caṇḍe ca nihate daitye muṇḍe ca vinipātite |bahuleṣu ca sainyeṣu kṣayiteṣvasureśvaraḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

चण्डे · च · निहते · दैत्ये · मुण्डे · च · विनिपातितेबहुलेषु · च · सैन्येषु · क्षयितेषु · असुरेश्वरः

caṇḍe · ca · nihate · daitye · muṇḍe · ca · vinipātitebahuleṣu · ca · sainyeṣu · kṣayiteṣu · asureśvaraḥ

Word by word

  1. चण्डेcaṇḍeCaṇḍam. loc. sg. of चण्ड; locative absolute with निहते
  2. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  3. निहतेnihatehaving been slainpast passive participle (kta) of नि + √हन् (adādi 2P ‘to strike, kill’), m. loc. sg.; predicate of the locative absolute
  4. दैत्येdaityethe Daityam. loc. sg. of दैत्य (taddhita: ‘descendant of Diti’); in apposition to चण्डे
  5. मुण्डेmuṇḍeMuṇḍam. loc. sg. of मुण्ड; second locative absolute
  6. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  7. विनिपातितेvinipātitehaving been struck downpast passive participle (kta) of the causative of वि + नि + √पत् (bhvādi 1P ‘to fall’), stem पातय- ‘to fell’; m. loc. sg.
  8. बहुलेषुbahuleṣumany, numerousm./n. loc. pl. of बहुल; qualifies सैन्येषु
  9. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  10. सैन्येषुsainyeṣuthe troops, the forcesm./n. loc. pl. of सैन्य (taddhita from सेना: ‘belonging to an army’, a soldier or a body of troops); third locative absolute
  11. क्षयितेषुkṣayiteṣuhaving been destroyedpast passive participle (kta) of the causative क्षयय- of √क्षि ‘to destroy’; m./n. loc. pl.; agrees with सैन्येषु
  12. असुरेश्वरःasureśvaraḥthe lord of the asuras (Śumbha)m. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of आदिदेश in 8.3

Translation

When the Daitya Caṇḍa had been slain and Muṇḍa struck down, and the many troops destroyed, the lord of the asuras —

In readable EnglishWhen the demon Caṇḍa had been slain and Muṇḍa struck down, and their many troops destroyed, the lord of the asuras —

The sentence runs on into 8.3, where the verb stands.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • क्षयितेष्वसुरेश्वरः kṣayiteṣvasureśvaraḥ → क्षयितेषु + असुरेश्वरः

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    Final u before a dissimilar vowel becomes v: क्षयितेषु + असुर- → क्षयितेष्वसुर-.

  • असुरेश्वरः asureśvaraḥ → असुर + ईश्वरः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + ī → e.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरेश्वरः asureśvaraḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    असुर + ईश्वर
    Vigraha
    असुराणाम् ईश्वरः asurāṇām īśvaraḥ
    Literally
    “lord of the asuras”
    In the verse
    Śumbha, named in the next verse
Morphology & anvaya

  • निहते nihate

    Locative absolute (sati saptamī): a noun and a participle in the locative state the circumstance — चण्डे निहते ‘Caṇḍa having been slain’. The verse strings three of them (Caṇḍa, Muṇḍa, the troops) before the subject appears.

  • विनिपातिते vinipātite

    Participle of a causative: पातय- ‘make fall’ + -इत. The same locative absolute, सिंहे च विनिपातिते, is in Śumbha’s order at 6.24.

  • क्षयितेषु kṣayiteṣu

    From the causative of √क्षि (क्षययति ‘destroys’); the same participle described Dhūmralocana’s army at 6.20 (बलं च क्षयितं कृत्स्नम्).

Anvaya · prose order

दैत्ये चण्डे निहते, मुण्डे च विनिपातिते, बहुलेषु सैन्येषु च क्षयितेषु, असुरेश्वरः (शुम्भः …)।

daitye caṇḍe nihate, muṇḍe ca vinipātite, bahuleṣu sainyeṣu ca kṣayiteṣu, asureśvaraḥ (śumbhaḥ …) |

Three locative absolutes give the situation; the subject असुरेश्वरः waits for its verb, आदिदेश, in 8.3.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

A summary of chapter 7: Caṇḍa was beheaded (7.20), Muṇḍa felled (7.21), and their army was destroyed or fled (7.22). Each word for the deaths is a past participle; the defeat is stated as already complete.

Traditional interpretation

The narrative pattern of the third carita is escalation: one general (Dhūmralocana, ch. 6), then two (Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa, ch. 7), now the whole host. Each defeat draws out a greater manifestation of the Goddess.

Verse 8.3

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ततः कोपपराधीनचेताः शुम्भः प्रतापवान्।उद्योगं सर्वसैन्यानां दैत्यानामादिदेश ह॥⁠३⁠॥

tataḥ kopaparādhīnacetāḥ śumbhaḥ pratāpavān |udyogaṃ sarvasainyānāṃ daityānāmādideśa ha ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ततः · कोपपराधीनचेताः · शुम्भः · प्रतापवान्उद्योगम् · सर्वसैन्यानाम् · दैत्यानाम् · आदिदेश · ह

tataḥ · kopaparādhīnacetāḥ · śumbhaḥ · pratāpavānudyogam · sarvasainyānām · daityānām · ādideśa · ha

Word by word

  1. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time, तद् + tas)
  2. कोपपराधीनचेताःkopaparādhīnacetāḥhis mind in the grip of angerm. nom. sg. of the s-stem bahuvrīhi कोपपराधीनचेतस्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies शुम्भः
  3. शुम्भःśumbhaḥŚumbham. nom. sg.; proper name; subject of आदिदेश
  4. प्रतापवान्pratāpavānthe mighty, the majesticm. nom. sg. of प्रतापवत् (प्रताप + matup, ‘possessing might’); qualifies शुम्भः
  5. उद्योगम्udyogamthe mobilization, the setting out to warm. acc. sg. of उद्योग (उद् + √युज्); object of आदिदेश
  6. सर्वसैन्यानाम्sarvasainyānāmof all the troopsm./n. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to दैत्यानाम्
  7. दैत्यानाम्daityānāmof the Daityasm. gen. pl. of दैत्य; depends on उद्योगम्
  8. आदिदेशādideśaorderedआ + √दिश् (tudādi 6P ‘to point out’; with आ ‘to command’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. हhaindeed (a narrative particle, untranslated)indeclinable (expletive particle of narration)

Translation

then Śumbha, the mighty, his mind dependent on anger, ordered the setting out of the Daityas, of all the troops.

In readable English— Śumbha, the mighty, his mind wholly mastered by anger, ordered all the Daitya armies to march.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • कोपपराधीनचेताः kopaparādhīnacetāḥ → कोप + पर + अधीन + चेताः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    पर + अधीन → पराधीन: a + a merge into ā.

  • उद्योगं सर्वसैन्यानां udyogaṃ sarvasainyānāṃ → उद्योगम् + सर्वसैन्यानाम्

    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; so too सर्वसैन्यानां before द.

  • दैत्यानामादिदेश daityānāmādideśa → दैत्यानाम् + आदिदेश

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    Before a vowel the m stays m and is written together with the next word.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • कोपपराधीनचेताः kopaparādhīnacetāḥ

    bahuvrīhi (its member पराधीन is itself a tatpuruṣa: परस्य अधीनम्, ‘dependent on another’)

    Members
    कोप + पराधीन + चेतस्
    Vigraha
    कोपस्य पराधीनं चेतः यस्य सः kopasya parādhīnaṃ cetaḥ yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “one whose mind is dependent on anger”
    In the verse
    Śumbha, mastered by his rage
    Alternative
    The first two members may also be read कोपेन पराधीनम् (‘made dependent by anger’); the sense is the same: anger, not Śumbha, is in control.

  • सर्वसैन्यानाम् sarvasainyānām

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    सर्व + सैन्य
    Vigraha
    सर्वाणि च तानि सैन्यानि च, तेषाम् sarvāṇi ca tāni sainyāni ca, teṣām
    Literally
    “of all the troops”
    In the verse
    the whole army, not a detachment
Morphology & anvaya

  • आदिदेश ādideśa

    Perfect of आ + √दिश्: reduplication दि-देश with guṇa of the root vowel in the singular. With आ it means ‘command’; the object उद्योगम् is the thing commanded.

  • कोपपराधीनचेताः kopaparādhīnacetāḥ

    An s-stem bahuvrīhi (-चेतस्) lengthens its vowel in the masculine nominative singular: -चेताः (like सुमनाः).

  • ह ha

    The narrative particle ह, common in Purāṇic past tense, adds no lexical meaning and is usually left untranslated.

Anvaya · prose order

ततः कोपपराधीनचेताः प्रतापवान् शुम्भः (असुरेश्वरः) सर्वसैन्यानां दैत्यानाम् उद्योगम् आदिदेश ह।

tataḥ kopaparādhīnacetāḥ pratāpavān śumbhaḥ (asureśvaraḥ) sarvasainyānāṃ daityānām udyogam ādideśa ha |

The subject of 8.2 (असुरेश्वरः) is now named: शुम्भः with two qualifiers; the verb आदिदेश governs उद्योगम्, with the double genitive saying whose mobilization.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Śumbha answers the loss of Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa with a total mobilization. The narrator stresses that the order comes from a mind not its own master (पराधीन).

Traditional interpretation

Through the Śumbha cycle the asura king acts from pride and anger (6.3 सक्रोधः, here कोपपराधीनचेताः), never from counsel; the tradition reads the asuras as passions that grow stronger the more they are opposed.

Modern reflection

A modern reflection: the compound’s own word for Śumbha’s state is पराधीन, ‘dependent on another’. The mighty king is, in that moment, not free; anger has taken the throne.

Verse 8.4

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

अद्य सर्वबलैर्दैत्याः षडशीतिरुदायुधाः।कम्बूनां चतुरशीतिर्निर्यान्तु स्वबलैर्वृताः॥⁠४⁠॥

adya sarvabalairdaityāḥ ṣaḍaśītirudāyudhāḥ |kambūnāṃ caturaśītirniryāntu svabalairvṛtāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

अद्य · सर्वबलैः · दैत्याः · षडशीतिः · उदायुधाःकम्बूनाम् · चतुरशीतिः · निर्यान्तु · स्वबलैः · वृताः

adya · sarvabalaiḥ · daityāḥ · ṣaḍaśītiḥ · udāyudhāḥkambūnām · caturaśītiḥ · niryāntu · svabalaiḥ · vṛtāḥ

Word by word

  1. अद्यadyatoday, nowindeclinable (adverb of time)
  2. सर्वबलैःsarvabalaiḥwith all (their) forcesn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument of accompaniment with निर्यान्तु
  3. दैत्याःdaityāḥthe Daityasm. nom. pl. of दैत्य; subject of the first clause
  4. षडशीतिःṣaḍaśītiḥeighty-six (clans)f. nom. sg. of the numeral षडशीति; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to दैत्याः
  5. उदायुधाःudāyudhāḥwith weapons raisedm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies दैत्याः
  6. कम्बूनाम्kambūnāmof the Kambus (an asura clan)m. gen. pl. of कम्बु (u-stem); depends on चतुरशीतिः
  7. चतुरशीतिःcaturaśītiḥeighty-four (clans)f. nom. sg. of the numeral चतुरशीति; compound (see Samāsa); subject of the second clause
  8. निर्यान्तुniryāntulet them go forth, march outनिस् + √या (adādi 2P ‘to go’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  9. स्वबलैःsvabalaiḥby their own forcesn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent with वृताः
  10. वृताःvṛtāḥsurroundedpast passive participle (kta) of √वृ (svādi 5 ‘to cover, surround’), m. nom. pl.

Translation

Today let the Daityas, eighty-six, with weapons raised, (go forth) with all their forces; let eighty-four of the Kambus go forth, surrounded by their own forces.

In readable English“Today let the eighty-six Daitya clans march out with all their forces, weapons raised, and the eighty-four clans of the Kambus, each surrounded by its own troops.

What is counted is not named; ‘clans’ is supplied from कुलानि in 8.5. The plural वृताः is construed with the eighty-four by sense.

Textual verification / uncertain: उदायुधाः may be an epithet (‘with weapons raised’) or a clan name (‘the Udāyudhas’), and the noun counted by षडशीतिः and चतुरशीतिः is not stated (clans, or chiefs). The translation follows the epithet reading and supplies ‘clans’ from 8.5.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सर्वबलैर्दैत्याः sarvabalairdaityāḥ → सर्वबलैः + दैत्याः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After a vowel other than a/ā, visarga (as ru) surfaces as r before a voiced consonant; so too स्वबलैर्वृताः and चतुरशीतिर्निर्यान्तु.

  • षडशीतिरुदायुधाः ṣaḍaśītirudāyudhāḥ → षडशीतिः + उदायुधाः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    -iḥ before a vowel → -ir.

  • षडशीति ṣaḍaśīti → षट् + अशीति

    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    षट् ‘six’ voices its final ṭ to ḍ before the vowel of अशीति.

  • निर्यान्तु niryāntu → निस् + यान्तु

    prefix sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    The s of the prefix निस् becomes r before the voiced y.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वबलैः sarvabalaiḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    सर्व + बल
    Vigraha
    सर्वाणि च तानि बलानि च, तैः sarvāṇi ca tāni balāni ca, taiḥ
    Literally
    “with all forces”
    In the verse
    with their entire strength

  • षडशीतिः ṣaḍaśītiḥ

    karmadhāraya (numeral compound of the śākapārthiva type, the middle word अधिका understood)

    Members
    षष् + अशीति
    Vigraha
    षडधिका अशीतिः ṣaḍadhikā aśītiḥ
    Literally
    “eighty increased by six”
    In the verse
    eighty-six

  • उदायुधाः udāyudhāḥ

    bahuvrīhi (prādi: the first member उद् stands for उद्यत)

    Members
    उद् + आयुध
    Vigraha
    उद्यतानि आयुधानि येषां ते udyatāni āyudhāni yeṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “those whose weapons are raised”
    In the verse
    armed and ready to strike
    Alternative
    Some take Udāyudha as the name of a clan, parallel to the Kambus of the next line: ‘the eighty-six Udāyudhas’. The adjective reading is chosen because it needs no name the text nowhere else uses.

  • चतुरशीतिः caturaśītiḥ

    karmadhāraya (numeral compound, as षडशीतिः)

    Members
    चतुर् + अशीति
    Vigraha
    चतुरधिका अशीतिः caturadhikā aśītiḥ
    Literally
    “eighty increased by four”
    In the verse
    eighty-four

  • स्वबलैः svabalaiḥ

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    स्व + बल
    Vigraha
    स्वानि बलानि, तैः svāni balāni, taiḥ
    Literally
    “with own forces”
    In the verse
    each clan with its own troops
Morphology & anvaya

  • षडशीतिः ṣaḍaśītiḥ

    Numerals from twenty upward are feminine singular nouns (विंशतिः, अशीतिः); they stand in apposition to what is counted or take it in the genitive. Both constructions are here: दैत्याः षडशीतिः, कम्बूनां चतुरशीतिः.

  • निर्यान्तु niryāntu

    Third-person imperative (loṭ) of √या: a command to absent subjects, ‘let them march’.

  • वृताः vṛtāḥ

    Past participle of √वृ ‘to cover’, used for an escort: ‘surrounded by’ with the instrumental.

Anvaya · prose order

अद्य उदायुधाः षडशीतिः दैत्याः सर्वबलैः (निर्यान्तु); कम्बूनां चतुरशीतिः स्वबलैः वृताः निर्यान्तु।

adya udāyudhāḥ ṣaḍaśītiḥ daityāḥ sarvabalaiḥ (niryāntu); kambūnāṃ caturaśītiḥ svabalaiḥ vṛtāḥ niryāntu |

Śumbha’s order in direct speech: two subjects, each a numeral, share the one imperative निर्यान्तु.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Śumbha’s command begins (8.4–6). He names asura clans by number; the list conveys the size of the host rather than an order of battle.

Traditional interpretation

The Kambus and the clans of 8.5–6 belong to the Purāṇic lore of the asura races; the text names them once and does not describe them again.

Verse 8.5

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

कोटिवीर्याणि पञ्चाशदसुराणां कुलानि वै।शतं कुलानि धौम्राणां निर्गच्छन्तु ममाज्ञया॥⁠५⁠॥

koṭivīryāṇi pañcāśadasurāṇāṃ kulāni vai |śataṃ kulāni dhaumrāṇāṃ nirgacchantu mamājñayā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

कोटिवीर्याणि · पञ्चाशत् · असुराणाम् · कुलानि · वैशतम् · कुलानि · धौम्राणाम् · निर्गच्छन्तु · मम · आज्ञया

koṭivīryāṇi · pañcāśat · asurāṇām · kulāni · vaiśatam · kulāni · dhaumrāṇām · nirgacchantu · mama · ājñayā

Word by word

  1. कोटिवीर्याणिkoṭivīryāṇiof crore-fold valour; the Koṭivīrya (clans)n. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies कुलानि
  2. पञ्चाशत्pañcāśatfiftyf. nom. sg. of the numeral पञ्चाशत् (t-stem); in apposition to कुलानि
  3. असुराणाम्asurāṇāmof the asurasm. gen. pl. of असुर; depends on कुलानि
  4. कुलानिkulānifamilies, clansn. nom. pl. of कुल; subject of निर्गच्छन्तु
  5. वैvaiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  6. शतम्śatama hundredn. nom. sg. of the numeral शत; in apposition to the second कुलानि
  7. कुलानिkulānifamilies, clansn. nom. pl. of कुल; second subject of निर्गच्छन्तु
  8. धौम्राणाम्dhaumrāṇāmof the Dhaumrasm. gen. pl. of धौम्र (taddhita from धूम्र: ‘descendants of Dhūmra’); depends on कुलानि
  9. निर्गच्छन्तुnirgacchantulet them go outनिस् + √गम् (bhvādi 1P, present stem गच्छ-), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  10. ममmamamyfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.; depends on आज्ञया
  11. आज्ञयाājñayāby the commandf. instr. sg. of आज्ञा (आ + √ज्ञा)

Translation

The fifty families of the asuras, of crore-fold valour, indeed — a hundred families of the Dhaumras — let them go out at my command.

In readable EnglishLet the fifty asura clans of the Koṭivīryas, and the hundred clans of the Dhaumras, march out at my command.

‘and’ is supplied between the two subjects.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • पञ्चाशदसुराणां pañcāśadasurāṇāṃ → पञ्चाशत् + असुराणाम्

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before a vowel; the final m of असुराणाम् becomes anusvāra before क (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • ममाज्ञया mamājñayā → मम + आज्ञया

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • शतं कुलानि śataṃ kulāni → शतम् + कुलानि

    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

  • निर्गच्छन्तु nirgacchantu → निस् + गच्छन्तु

    prefix sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    The s of निस् becomes r before the voiced g, as in निर्यान्तु (8.4).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • कोटिवीर्याणि koṭivīryāṇi

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    कोटि + वीर्य
    Vigraha
    कोटिः वीर्याणि येषां तानि koṭiḥ vīryāṇi yeṣāṃ tāni
    Literally
    “(clans) whose valour is a crore”
    In the verse
    the Koṭivīryas, clans of immense might
    Alternative
    Taken by many as the name of a clan (Koṭivīrya) rather than a description; as a bahuvrīhi agreeing with कुलानि it serves as both: ‘the fifty Koṭivīrya clans’.
Morphology & anvaya

  • पञ्चाशत् pañcāśat

    A feminine t-stem numeral, used in the singular whatever the number counted: पञ्चाशत् … कुलानि ‘fifty clans’.

  • धौम्राणाम् dhaumrāṇām

    Vṛddhi taddhita (धूम्र → धौम्र) forming a patronymic or clan name: ‘those of Dhūmra’s line’.

  • निर्गच्छन्तु nirgacchantu

    Imperative third plural of निस् + √गम्, with the present stem गच्छ-; parallel to निर्यान्तु (8.4, 8.6).

Anvaya · prose order

असुराणां कोटिवीर्याणि पञ्चाशत् कुलानि, धौम्राणां शतं कुलानि (च) मम आज्ञया वै निर्गच्छन्तु।

asurāṇāṃ koṭivīryāṇi pañcāśat kulāni, dhaumrāṇāṃ śataṃ kulāni (ca) mama ājñayā vai nirgacchantu |

Two numbered subjects (पञ्चाशत् कुलानि, शतं कुलानि) share the imperative निर्गच्छन्तु; मम आज्ञया gives the authority.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The muster continues: fifty Koṭivīrya clans and a hundred Dhaumra clans. The phrase मम आज्ञया makes the order personal; it returns in the next verse.

Traditional interpretation

Dhaumra is formed from Dhūmra, ‘smoke-coloured’, the word that named Dhūmralocana (‘Smoke-eye’), slain by a mere हुंकार of Ambikā (6.13). Whether the text means his kin is not stated; the name may simply mark another asura line.

Verse 8.6

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

कालका दौर्हृदा मौर्याः कालकेयास्तथासुराः।युद्धाय सज्जा निर्यान्तु आज्ञया त्वरिता मम॥⁠६⁠॥

kālakā daurhṛdā mauryāḥ kālakeyāstathāsurāḥ |yuddhāya sajjā niryāntu ājñayā tvaritā mama ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

कालकाः · दौर्हृदाः · मौर्याः · कालकेयाः · तथा · असुराःयुद्धाय · सज्जाः · निर्यान्तु · आज्ञया · त्वरिताः · मम

kālakāḥ · daurhṛdāḥ · mauryāḥ · kālakeyāḥ · tathā · asurāḥyuddhāya · sajjāḥ · niryāntu · ājñayā · tvaritāḥ · mama

Word by word

  1. कालकाःkālakāḥthe Kālakas (an asura clan)m. nom. pl. of कालक; subject of निर्यान्तु
  2. दौर्हृदाःdaurhṛdāḥthe Daurhṛdas (an asura clan; the name means ‘of the ill-hearted’)m. nom. pl. of दौर्हृद (vṛddhi taddhita from दुर्हृद् ‘ill-hearted, hostile’)
  3. मौर्याःmauryāḥthe Mauryas (an asura clan)m. nom. pl. of मौर्य
  4. कालकेयाःkālakeyāḥthe Kālakeyas (an asura clan, ‘sons of Kālakā’)m. nom. pl. of कालकेय (taddhita -एय, a metronymic from कालका)
  5. तथाtathālikewise, andindeclinable (adverb of manner, used as a conjunction)
  6. असुराःasurāḥthe asurasm. nom. pl. of असुर; sums up the list
  7. युद्धायyuddhāyafor battlen. dat. sg. of युद्ध (√युध् + kta); dative of purpose
  8. सज्जाःsajjāḥready, equippedm. nom. pl. of the adjective सज्ज; predicate with निर्यान्तु
  9. निर्यान्तुniryāntulet them go forthनिस् + √या (adādi 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  10. आज्ञयाājñayāat the commandf. instr. sg. of आज्ञा
  11. त्वरिताःtvaritāḥin haste, swiftlypast participle (kta) of √त्वर् (bhvādi 1Ā ‘to hurry’), m. nom. pl.; used adverbially
  12. ममmamamyfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, gen. sg.; depends on आज्ञया

Translation

The Kālakas, the Daurhṛdas, the Mauryas, the Kālakeyas, and likewise the asuras — ready for battle let them go forth, hastening at my command.

In readable EnglishLet the Kālakas, the Daurhṛdas, the Mauryas and the Kālakeyas — every asura — march out at once at my command, armed for battle.”

तथासुराः is rendered as summing up the list (‘every asura’); it may also mean ‘and the other asuras’.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • कालका दौर्हृदा मौर्याः kālakā daurhṛdā mauryāḥ → कालकाः + दौर्हृदाः + मौर्याः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)

    -āḥ before a voiced consonant: the visarga (as ru) becomes y after ā, and that y is then dropped before a consonant, leaving -ā. So too युद्धाय सज्जा निर्यान्तु and त्वरिता मम.

  • कालकेयास्तथासुराः kālakeyāstathāsurāḥ → कालकेयाः + तथा + असुराः

    visarga sandhi and vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    Visarga before t becomes s (कालकेयास्तथा); ā + a → ā (तथासुराः).

Left unjoined

  • निर्यान्तु | आज्ञया — Hiatus at the pāda boundary: निर्यान्तु closes the third pāda and आज्ञया opens the fourth. Joining them (निर्यान्त्वाज्ञया) would erase the pāda break; the verse, like much epic and Purāṇic verse, leaves vowels unjoined across a pāda boundary (a metrical licence). The Gita Press text prints them apart.
Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • कालकेयाः kālakeyāḥ

    The suffix -एय forms metronymics (descent through the mother): कालकेय ‘son of Kālakā’, as कौन्तेय ‘son of Kuntī’.

  • त्वरिताः tvaritāḥ

    A past participle in agreement with the subject but adverbial in sense: ‘let them go forth, hastened’ = ‘let them go at once’.

Anvaya · prose order

कालकाः दौर्हृदाः मौर्याः कालकेयाः तथा (अन्ये) असुराः युद्धाय सज्जाः मम आज्ञया त्वरिताः निर्यान्तु।

kālakāḥ daurhṛdāḥ mauryāḥ kālakeyāḥ tathā (anye) asurāḥ yuddhāya sajjāḥ mama ājñayā tvaritāḥ niryāntu |

A list of clans sums up in असुराः; the imperative निर्यान्तु has two predicate adjectives (सज्जाः, त्वरिताः) and the instrumental of authority, मम आज्ञया.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The muster ends with four more asura clans and the repeated मम आज्ञया. The haste (त्वरिताः) matches Śumbha’s anger in 8.3.

Traditional interpretation

The Kālakeyas are known from the Mahābhārata: they are the asuras who hide in the ocean until Agastya drinks it dry, and Arjuna later fights them with the Paulomas. The Devī Māhātmya draws its enemies from the same old lineages of the asuras.

Verse 8.7

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

इत्याज्ञाप्यासुरपतिः शुम्भो भैरवशासनः।निर्जगाम महासैन्यसहस्रैर्बहुभिर्वृतः॥⁠७⁠॥

ityājñāpyāsurapatiḥ śumbho bhairavaśāsanaḥ |nirjagāma mahāsainyasahasrairbahubhirvṛtaḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

इति · आज्ञाप्य · असुरपतिः · शुम्भः · भैरवशासनःनिर्जगाम · महासैन्यसहस्रैः · बहुभिः · वृतः

iti · ājñāpya · asurapatiḥ · śumbhaḥ · bhairavaśāsanaḥnirjagāma · mahāsainyasahasraiḥ · bahubhiḥ · vṛtaḥ

Word by word

  1. इतिitithus (closing the speech)indeclinable (particle of quotation)
  2. आज्ञाप्यājñāpyahaving commandedabsolutive (lyap) of the causative of आ + √ज्ञा (kryādi 9P ‘to know’; causative आज्ञापय- ‘to command’)
  3. असुरपतिःasurapatiḥthe lord of the asurasm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to शुम्भः
  4. शुम्भःśumbhaḥŚumbham. nom. sg.; subject of निर्जगाम
  5. भैरवशासनःbhairavaśāsanaḥwhose commands are terriblem. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies शुम्भः
  6. निर्जगामnirjagāmawent out, set forthनिस् + √गम् (bhvādi 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. महासैन्यसहस्रैःmahāsainyasahasraiḥby thousands of great troopsn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent with वृतः
  8. बहुभिःbahubhiḥmanyn. instr. pl. of बहु (u-stem); qualifies महासैन्यसहस्रैः
  9. वृतःvṛtaḥsurroundedpast passive participle (kta) of √वृ (svādi 5), m. nom. sg.; qualifies शुम्भः

Translation

Having commanded thus, Śumbha, the lord of the asuras, of terrible command, went out, surrounded by many thousands of great troops.

In readable EnglishHaving given these orders, Śumbha, lord of the asuras, whose commands were dreaded, set out, surrounded by many thousands of mighty troops.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • इत्याज्ञाप्यासुरपतिः ityājñāpyāsurapatiḥ → इति + आज्ञाप्य + असुरपतिः

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ and savarṇa-dīrgha) · इको यणचि (6.1.77); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    i before ā becomes y (इत्याज्ञाप्य); a + a → ā (आज्ञाप्यासुर-).

  • शुम्भो भैरवशासनः śumbho bhairavaśāsanaḥ → शुम्भः + भैरवशासनः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before a voiced consonant becomes -o.

  • सहस्रैर्बहुभिर्वृतः sahasrairbahubhirvṛtaḥ → सहस्रैः + बहुभिः + वृतः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After ai and i the visarga surfaces as r before the voiced b and v.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरपतिः asurapatiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    असुर + पति
    Vigraha
    असुराणां पतिः asurāṇāṃ patiḥ
    Literally
    “lord of the asuras”
    In the verse
    Śumbha as king

  • भैरवशासनः bhairavaśāsanaḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    भैरव + शासन
    Vigraha
    भैरवं शासनं यस्य सः bhairavaṃ śāsanaṃ yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose command is terrible”
    In the verse
    a ruler whose orders are feared

  • महासैन्यसहस्रैः mahāsainyasahasraiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (its first member महासैन्य is a karmadhāraya: महत् च तत् सैन्यं च)

    Members
    महासैन्य + सहस्र
    Vigraha
    महासैन्यानां सहस्राणि, तैः mahāsainyānāṃ sahasrāṇi, taiḥ
    Literally
    “by thousands of great troops”
    In the verse
    by an immense host
Morphology & anvaya

  • आज्ञाप्य ājñāpya

    Absolutive of a causative with a prefix: the stem आज्ञापय- drops -अय before the suffix -य (lyap, used instead of -त्वा when a prefix is present).

  • निर्जगाम nirjagāma

    Perfect of √गम्: the reduplicated syllable has j for g (ज-गाम), with vṛddhi of the root vowel in the third singular.

Anvaya · prose order

इति आज्ञाप्य भैरवशासनः असुरपतिः शुम्भः बहुभिः महासैन्यसहस्रैः वृतः निर्जगाम।

iti ājñāpya bhairavaśāsanaḥ asurapatiḥ śumbhaḥ bahubhiḥ mahāsainyasahasraiḥ vṛtaḥ nirjagāma |

An absolutive clause (इति आज्ञाप्य), the subject with two qualifiers, and the main verb निर्जगाम with the participle phrase बहुभिः … वृतः.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Śumbha now leaves his city with the host. Until now he had sent others: the messenger Sugrīva (5.102), Dhūmralocana (6.3), Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa.

Traditional interpretation

Śumbha does not meet the Goddess in person until chapter 9 (9.8, 9.17); here he is the commander who sets the whole host in motion, and the narrative turns at once to the Goddess’s answer.

Verse 8.8

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

आयान्तं चण्डिका दृष्ट्वा तत्सैन्यमतिभीषणम्।ज्यास्वनैः पूरयामास धरणीगगनान्तरम्॥⁠८⁠॥

āyāntaṃ caṇḍikā dṛṣṭvā tatsainyamatibhīṣaṇam |jyāsvanaiḥ pūrayāmāsa dharaṇīgaganāntaram ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

आयान्तम् · चण्डिका · दृष्ट्वा · तत्सैन्यम् · अतिभीषणम्ज्यास्वनैः · पूरयामास · धरणीगगनान्तरम्

āyāntam · caṇḍikā · dṛṣṭvā · tatsainyam · atibhīṣaṇamjyāsvanaiḥ · pūrayāmāsa · dharaṇīgaganāntaram

Word by word

  1. आयान्तम्āyāntamcoming, approaching (him, Śumbha)present participle (śatṛ) of आ + √या (adādi 2P), m. acc. sg.; object of दृष्ट्वा
  2. चण्डिकाcaṇḍikāCaṇḍikā, the Fierce Onef. nom. sg. of चण्डिका; subject
  3. दृष्ट्वाdṛṣṭvāhaving seenabsolutive (ktvā) of √दृश् (bhvādi 1P ‘to see’)
  4. तत्सैन्यम्tatsainyamhis armyn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); second object of दृष्ट्वा
  5. अतिभीषणम्atibhīṣaṇamexceedingly terriblen. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies तत्सैन्यम्
  6. ज्यास्वनैःjyāsvanaiḥwith the twangs of (her) bowstringm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument of पूरयामास
  7. पूरयामासpūrayāmāsafilledcausative of √पॄ (‘to fill’), stem पूरय-; periphrastic perfect (liṭ with आम्), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  8. धरणीगगनान्तरम्dharaṇīgaganāntaramthe space between earth and skyn. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of पूरयामास

Translation

Caṇḍikā, having seen (him) approaching, and his exceedingly terrible army, filled the space between earth and sky with the twangs of her bowstring.

In readable EnglishSeeing Śumbha approach with his dreadful army, Caṇḍikā filled the space between earth and sky with the twanging of her bowstring.

‘Śumbha’ is supplied from the masculine participle आयान्तम्; see Samāsa.

Textual verification / uncertain: आयान्तम् is masculine; most renderings take it loosely with the army (‘seeing that army approaching’). The analysis reads it strictly, of Śumbha, who set out in 8.7.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • आयान्तं चण्डिका āyāntaṃ caṇḍikā → आयान्तम् + चण्डिका

    anusvāra · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.

  • तत्सैन्यमतिभीषणम् tatsainyamatibhīṣaṇam → तत्सैन्यम् + अतिभीषणम्

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    The m is kept and written together with the vowel.

  • धरणीगगनान्तरम् dharaṇīgaganāntaram → धरणीगगन + अन्तरम्

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • तत्सैन्यम् tatsainyam

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    तद् + सैन्य
    Vigraha
    तस्य सैन्यम् tasya sainyam
    Literally
    “the army of him”
    In the verse
    Śumbha’s host
    Alternative
    Read as two words, तत् सैन्यम् ‘that army’. But आयान्तम् is masculine and cannot agree with the neuter सैन्यम्; it must mean Śumbha himself, and तत्सैन्यम् ‘his army’ then completes the picture.

  • अतिभीषणम् atibhīṣaṇam

    prādi tatpuruṣa (karmadhāraya with the prefix अति ‘exceedingly’)

    Members
    अति + भीषण
    Vigraha
    अतिशयेन भीषणम् atiśayena bhīṣaṇam
    Literally
    “exceedingly terrible”
    In the verse
    dreadful to see

  • ज्यास्वनैः jyāsvanaiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    ज्या + स्वन
    Vigraha
    ज्यायाः स्वनाः, तैः jyāyāḥ svanāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally
    “with the sounds of the bowstring”
    In the verse
    with the twanging of her bow

  • धरणीगगनान्तरम् dharaṇīgaganāntaram

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa whose first member is an itaretara dvandva

    Members
    धरणीगगन + अन्तर
    Vigraha
    धरणी च गगनं च धरणीगगने; तयोः अन्तरम् dharaṇī ca gaganaṃ ca dharaṇīgagane; tayoḥ antaram
    Literally
    “the interval of earth and sky”
    In the verse
    all the space between ground and heaven
Morphology & anvaya

  • आयान्तम् āyāntam

    Present participle आयत् (आ + √या + śatṛ), masculine accusative singular with the strong stem आयान्त्-. Its gender shows that the one ‘coming’ is Śumbha, not the (neuter) army.

  • पूरयामास pūrayāmāsa

    Periphrastic perfect: causative and other derived stems form the perfect with -आम् and a perfect of √अस् (पूरय् + आम् + आस).

Anvaya · prose order

चण्डिका आयान्तं (शुम्भम्) अतिभीषणं तत्सैन्यं (च) दृष्ट्वा ज्यास्वनैः धरणीगगनान्तरं पूरयामास।

caṇḍikā āyāntaṃ (śumbham) atibhīṣaṇaṃ tatsainyaṃ (ca) dṛṣṭvā jyāsvanaiḥ dharaṇīgaganāntaraṃ pūrayāmāsa |

An absolutive clause with two objects (आयान्तम्, तत्सैन्यम्), then the main verb पूरयामास with its instrument and object.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The Goddess answers the muster with sound before any weapon is loosed. The bowstring’s twang filling heaven and earth recalls her first battle, where the same sound shook the underworlds (2.38, धनुर्ज्यानिःस्वनेन).

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Caṇḍikā is also a name in the Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa), name 755, चण्डिका. In the Devī Māhātmya it is the commonest name of the Goddess in battle.

Verse 8.9

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ततः सिंहो महानादमतीव कृतवान् नृप।घण्टास्वनेन तन्नादमम्बिका चोपबृंहयत्॥⁠९⁠॥

tataḥ siṃho mahānādamatīva kṛtavān nṛpa |ghaṇṭāsvanena tannādamambikā copabṛṃhayat ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ततः · सिंहः · महानादम् · अतीव · कृतवान् · नृपघण्टास्वनेन · तन्नादम् · अम्बिका · च · उपबृंहयत्

tataḥ · siṃhaḥ · mahānādam · atīva · kṛtavān · nṛpaghaṇṭāsvanena · tannādam · ambikā · ca · upabṛṃhayat

Word by word

  1. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable (adverb of time)
  2. सिंहःsiṃhaḥthe lion (the Goddess’s mount)m. nom. sg. of सिंह; subject of कृतवान्
  3. महानादम्mahānādama great roarm. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of कृतवान्
  4. अतीवatīvaexceedingly, mightilyindeclinable (adverb, अति + इव)
  5. कृतवान्kṛtavānmade, let outpast active participle (ktavatu) of √कृ (tanādi 8 ‘to do’), m. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
  6. नृपnṛpaO king (Suratha)m. voc. sg. of नृप
  7. घण्टास्वनेनghaṇṭāsvanenawith the sound of her bellm. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument of उपबृंहयत्
  8. तन्नादम्tannādamthat roar of his (the lion’s)m. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); object of उपबृंहयत्
  9. अम्बिकाambikāAmbikā, the Motherf. nom. sg. of अम्बिका; subject of उपबृंहयत्
  10. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  11. उपबृंहयत्upabṛṃhayatswelled, augmentedउप + causative of √बृह् (‘to grow’; stem बृंहय- ‘to make great’), laṅ (imperfect) without the augment, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

Translation

Then the lion made an exceedingly great roar, O king, and Ambikā swelled that roar of his with the sound of her bell.

In readable EnglishThen, O king, the lion gave a mighty roar, and Ambikā swelled that roar with the clanging of her bell.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • सिंहो महानादमतीव siṃho mahānādamatīva → सिंहः + महानादम् + अतीव

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced m becomes -o; the m of महानादम् is kept before the vowel of अतीव.

  • अतीव atīva → अति + इव

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    i + i → ī; the fused adverb is written as one word.

  • तन्नादम् tannādam → तद् + नादम्

    consonant sandhi inside a compound (nasalisation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    A stop before a nasal may become the nasal of its class: d before n → n.

  • चोपबृंहयत् copabṛṃhayat → च + उपबृंहयत्

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + u → o.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • महानादम् mahānādam

    karmadhāraya

    Members
    महत् + नाद
    Vigraha
    महान् चासौ नादः च, तम् mahān cāsau nādaḥ ca, tam
    Literally
    “a great roar”
    In the verse
    a mighty roar

  • घण्टास्वनेन ghaṇṭāsvanena

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    घण्टा + स्वन
    Vigraha
    घण्टायाः स्वनः, तेन ghaṇṭāyāḥ svanaḥ, tena
    Literally
    “with the sound of the bell”
    In the verse
    with the clanging of her bell

  • तन्नादम् tannādam

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    तद् + नाद
    Vigraha
    तस्य नादः, तम् tasya nādaḥ, tam
    Literally
    “the roar of it”
    In the verse
    the lion’s roar
    Alternative
    Not two words: a separate तत् is neuter and could not agree with the masculine नादम्.
Morphology & anvaya

  • कृतवान् kṛtavān

    The past active participle (-तवत्) is often used for a finite past: सिंहः … कृतवान् ‘the lion made’.

  • उपबृंहयत् upabṛṃhayat

    The regular imperfect would be उपाबृंहयत् (the augment अ stands between prefix and verb). The augmentless form is an epic licence (or an injunctive with past sense); the meaning is unaffected.

  • नृप nṛpa

    Vocative: Medhas addresses King Suratha, his listener.

Anvaya · prose order

नृप, ततः सिंहः अतीव महानादं कृतवान्; अम्बिका च घण्टास्वनेन तन्नादम् उपबृंहयत्।

nṛpa, tataḥ siṃhaḥ atīva mahānādaṃ kṛtavān; ambikā ca ghaṇṭāsvanena tannādam upabṛṃhayat |

Two clauses joined by च: the lion’s roar, and Ambikā’s bell amplifying it.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The second and third sounds join the bowstring of 8.8: the lion’s roar and the bell. Ambikā and Caṇḍikā are the same Goddess under two names.

Traditional interpretation

The bell was Indra’s gift, taken from Airāvata (2.22); in the first battle its sound bewildered the asuras (2.56), and the gods pray to be guarded by it (4.24; 11.27). Its sound is itself a weapon that weakens the enemy.

Verse 8.10

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानां नादापूरितदिङ्मुखा।निनादैर्भीषणैः काली जिग्ये विस्तारितानना॥⁠१०⁠॥

dhanurjyāsiṃhaghaṇṭānāṃ nādāpūritadiṅmukhā |ninādairbhīṣaṇaiḥ kālī jigye vistāritānanā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानाम् · नादापूरितदिङ्मुखानिनादैः · भीषणैः · काली · जिग्ये · विस्तारितानना

dhanurjyāsiṃhaghaṇṭānām · nādāpūritadiṅmukhāninādaiḥ · bhīṣaṇaiḥ · kālī · jigye · vistāritānanā

Word by word

  1. धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानाम्dhanurjyāsiṃhaghaṇṭānāmof the bowstring, the lion and the bellf. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on the member नाद- of the next word
  2. नादापूरितदिङ्मुखाnādāpūritadiṅmukhāthe quarters (around her) filled with the soundf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies काली
  3. निनादैःninādaiḥwith roarsm. instr. pl. of निनाद; instrument of जिग्ये
  4. भीषणैःbhīṣaṇaiḥterriblem. instr. pl. of भीषण; qualifies निनादैः
  5. कालीkālīKālīf. nom. sg. of काली; subject
  6. जिग्येjigyeprevailed, outdid (them)√जि (bhvādi 1P ‘to conquer’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, with an ātmanepada ending (the regular active is जिगाय)
  7. विस्तारिताननाvistāritānanāher mouth stretched widef. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies काली

Translation

Kālī — the faces of the quarters about her filled with the sound of bowstring, lion and bell — her mouth stretched wide, prevailed with terrible roars.

In readable EnglishWith the twang of the bow, the lion’s roar and the clang of the bell filling every quarter, Kālī, her mouth gaping wide, outdid them all with her terrible roars.

The object of ‘outdid’ (the three sounds) is supplied; जिग्ये is taken in its active sense.

Textual verification / uncertain: The construction is loose: the genitive धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानाम् hangs on the member नाद- of a compound, and जिग्ये is an ātmanepada perfect of a parasmaipada root. The reading ‘Kālī outdid those sounds with her roars’ is supported by the parallel at 9.22 and 9.26; ‘Kālī, who filled the quarters with the sound …, prevailed with her roars’ is also possible.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • धनुर्ज्या dhanurjyā → धनुस् + ज्या

    visarga sandhi inside a compound · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    The s of धनुस् becomes r before the voiced j.

  • नादापूरित nādāpūrita → नाद + आपूरित

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā; so too विस्तारित + आनना → विस्तारितानना.

  • दिङ्मुखा diṅmukhā → दिश् + मुखा

    consonant sandhi inside a compound (nasalisation) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    दिश् first takes the form दिक् at the end of a member; k before the nasal m becomes ṅ.

  • निनादैर्भीषणैः ninādairbhīṣaṇaiḥ → निनादैः + भीषणैः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After ai the visarga surfaces as r before the voiced bh.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानाम् dhanurjyāsiṃhaghaṇṭānām

    itaretara dvandva (its first member a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa)

    Members
    धनुर्ज्या + सिंह + घण्टा
    Vigraha
    धनुर्ज्या च सिंहः च घण्टा च, तेषाम् (धनुर्ज्या = धनुषः ज्या) dhanurjyā ca siṃhaḥ ca ghaṇṭā ca, teṣām (dhanurjyā = dhanuṣaḥ jyā)
    Literally
    “of bowstring, lion and bell”
    In the verse
    the three sounds of 8.8–9

  • नादापूरितदिङ्मुखा nādāpūritadiṅmukhā

    bahuvrīhi (containing a tṛtīyā relation नादेन आपूरित and a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa दिङ्मुख)

    Members
    नाद + आपूरित + दिङ्मुख
    Vigraha
    नादेन आपूरितानि दिङ्मुखानि यस्याः (परितः) सा (दिङ्मुखानि = दिशां मुखानि) nādena āpūritāni diṅmukhāni yasyāḥ (paritaḥ) sā (diṅmukhāni = diśāṃ mukhāni)
    Literally
    “she around whom the faces of the quarters are filled with sound”
    In the verse
    Kālī amid the din that fills every direction
    Alternative
    As in 7.8, where the same epithet describes Kālī, it can mean ‘she by whom the quarters are filled with sound’ (नादेन आपूरितानि दिङ्मुखानि यया सा). Here the sound is said to be that of the bowstring, lion and bell, which are not Kālī’s, so the looser ‘around whom’ is chosen. The genitive धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानाम् is construed with the member नाद- (an epic licence).

  • विस्तारितानना vistāritānanā

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    विस्तारित + आनन
    Vigraha
    विस्तारितम् आननं यया सा vistāritam ānanaṃ yayā sā
    Literally
    “she by whom the mouth is stretched wide”
    In the verse
    with gaping mouth (cf. अतिविस्तारवदना, 7.8)
Morphology & anvaya

  • जिग्ये jigye

    Perfect of √जि with an ātmanepada ending. √जि is parasmaipada (जिगाय); जिग्ये could formally be a passive perfect (‘was conquered’), but with काली as subject and the instrumental निनादैः the sense is active: ‘she prevailed (over those sounds) with her roars’.

  • विस्तारितानना vistāritānanā

    विस्तारित is the past participle of the causative of वि + √स्तृ ‘to spread’: ‘made wide’.

Anvaya · prose order

धनुर्ज्यासिंहघण्टानां नादापूरितदिङ्मुखा विस्तारितानना काली भीषणैः निनादैः जिग्ये।

dhanurjyāsiṃhaghaṇṭānāṃ nādāpūritadiṅmukhā vistāritānanā kālī bhīṣaṇaiḥ ninādaiḥ jigye |

The subject काली carries two bahuvrīhis; the verb जिग्ये has the instrument भीषणैः निनादैः. What she outdid — the sounds just named — is understood.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The fourth sound, Kālī’s roar, tops the other three. Both epithets repeat her portrait at her birth (7.8: नादापूरितदिङ्मुखा, अतिविस्तारवदना).

Traditional interpretation

Before the armies close, the battle is fought in sound. The scene returns at Śumbha’s own onset (9.19–22): conch, bowstring, bell and lion fill the quarters, then Kālī strikes earth and sky and ‘the earlier sounds were drowned out’ (प्राक्स्वनास्ते तिरोहिताः, 9.22). At 9.26 √जि is again used of one sound overcoming another (जितवान्), which supports the reading of जिग्ये here.

Verse 8.11

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

तं निनादमुपश्रुत्य दैत्यसैन्यैश्चतुर्दिशम्।देवी सिंहस्तथा काली सरोषैः परिवारिताः॥⁠११⁠॥

taṃ ninādamupaśrutya daityasainyaiścaturdiśam |devī siṃhastathā kālī saroṣaiḥ parivāritāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

तम् · निनादम् · उपश्रुत्य · दैत्यसैन्यैः · चतुर्दिशम्देवी · सिंहः · तथा · काली · सरोषैः · परिवारिताः

tam · ninādam · upaśrutya · daityasainyaiḥ · caturdiśamdevī · siṃhaḥ · tathā · kālī · saroṣaiḥ · parivāritāḥ

Word by word

  1. तम्tamthatpronoun तद्, m. acc. sg.; qualifies निनादम्
  2. निनादम्ninādamroar, dinm. acc. sg. of निनाद; object of उपश्रुत्य
  3. उपश्रुत्यupaśrutyahaving heardabsolutive (lyap) of उप + √श्रु (svādi 5P ‘to hear’)
  4. दैत्यसैन्यैःdaityasainyaiḥby the Daitya armiesn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); agent of the passive परिवारिताः
  5. चतुर्दिशम्caturdiśamon all four sidescompound (see Samāsa), n. acc. sg. used adverbially
  6. देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg. of देवी; subject
  7. सिंहःsiṃhaḥthe lionm. nom. sg. of सिंह; subject
  8. तथाtathāand alsoindeclinable (used as a conjunction)
  9. कालीkālīKālīf. nom. sg. of काली; subject
  10. सरोषैःsaroṣaiḥenraged, furiousn. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies दैत्यसैन्यैः
  11. परिवारिताःparivāritāḥwere surroundedpast passive participle (kta) of the causative of परि + √वृ (‘to surround’), f. nom. pl.; predicate (supply आसन्)

Translation

Having heard that roar, by the enraged Daitya armies on four sides the Goddess, the lion and also Kālī were surrounded.

In readable EnglishHearing that roar, the furious Daitya armies closed in on every side around the Goddess, the lion and Kālī.

The passive is turned into an active sentence.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तं निनादमुपश्रुत्य taṃ ninādamupaśrutya → तम् + निनादम् + उपश्रुत्य

    anusvāra; final m before a vowel · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    तम् before n becomes तं; निनादम् before the vowel of उपश्रुत्य keeps its m.

  • दैत्यसैन्यैश्चतुर्दिशम् daityasainyaiścaturdiśam → दैत्यसैन्यैः + चतुर्दिशम्

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś.

  • सिंहस्तथा siṃhastathā → सिंहः + तथा

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • दैत्यसैन्यैः daityasainyaiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    दैत्य + सैन्य
    Vigraha
    दैत्यानां सैन्यानि, तैः daityānāṃ sainyāni, taiḥ
    Literally
    “by the armies of the Daityas”
    In the verse
    by Śumbha’s host

  • चतुर्दिशम् caturdiśam

    dvigu (samāhāra), used adverbially

    Members
    चतुर् + दिश्
    Vigraha
    चतसृणां दिशां समाहारः catasṛṇāṃ diśāṃ samāhāraḥ
    Literally
    “the group of four quarters”
    In the verse
    on every side
    Alternative
    Also treated as an avyayībhāva, ‘in the four quarters’; the adverbial sense is the same.

  • सरोषैः saroṣaiḥ

    bahuvrīhi (saha-bahuvrīhi, स for सह)

    Members
    स + रोष
    Vigraha
    रोषेण सह वर्तन्ते इति सरोषाणि, तैः roṣeṇa saha vartante iti saroṣāṇi, taiḥ
    Literally
    “with anger”
    In the verse
    furious
Morphology & anvaya

  • परिवारिताः parivāritāḥ

    The participle is feminine plural although the lion is masculine. Strict grammar lets the masculine prevail in a mixed group; the feminine here follows the two goddesses who head the list (an epic licence of agreement).

  • उपश्रुत्य upaśrutya

    In a passive sentence the absolutive goes with the agent in the instrumental: it is the Daitya armies who heard the roar and then surrounded the three.

Anvaya · prose order

तं निनादम् उपश्रुत्य सरोषैः दैत्यसैन्यैः देवी सिंहः तथा काली चतुर्दिशं परिवारिताः (आसन्)।

taṃ ninādam upaśrutya saroṣaiḥ daityasainyaiḥ devī siṃhaḥ tathā kālī caturdiśaṃ parivāritāḥ (āsan) |

A passive sentence: the subjects (देवी, सिंहः, काली) with the participle परिवारिताः; the agent दैत्यसैन्यैः also does the hearing (उपश्रुत्य).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The din draws the whole enemy host in, and the three — the Goddess, her lion and Kālī — are encircled. The situation is set for the help that arrives in the next verse.

Traditional interpretation

The three on the Goddess’s side are the Goddess, her mount and Kālī, the power that sprang from her own brow (7.6). The Mothers who now join them are a further multiplication of the one power facing the many.

Verse 8.12

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

एतस्मिन्नन्तरे भूप विनाशाय सुरद्विषाम्।भवायामरसिंहानामतिवीर्यबलान्विताः॥⁠१२⁠॥

etasminnantare bhūpa vināśāya suradviṣām |bhavāyāmarasiṃhānāmativīryabalānvitāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

एतस्मिन् · अन्तरे · भूप · विनाशाय · सुरद्विषाम्भवाय · अमरसिंहानाम् · अतिवीर्यबलान्विताः

etasmin · antare · bhūpa · vināśāya · suradviṣāmbhavāya · amarasiṃhānām · ativīryabalānvitāḥ

Word by word

  1. एतस्मिन्etasminin thispronoun एतद्, n. loc. sg.; qualifies अन्तरे
  2. अन्तरेantareinterval, momentn. loc. sg. of अन्तर; locative of time: ‘at this juncture’
  3. भूपbhūpaO king (Suratha)m. voc. sg. of भूप (‘protector of the earth’)
  4. विनाशायvināśāyafor the destructionm. dat. sg. of विनाश; dative of purpose
  5. सुरद्विषाम्suradviṣāmof the enemies of the godsm. gen. pl. of the root-noun compound सुरद्विष्; compound (see Samāsa); depends on विनाशाय
  6. भवायbhavāyafor the well-being, for the prosperitym. dat. sg. of भव; dative of purpose
  7. अमरसिंहानाम्amarasiṃhānāmof the lion-like immortals, of the foremost godsm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on भवाय
  8. अतिवीर्यबलान्विताःativīryabalānvitāḥendowed with surpassing valour and strengthf. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies शक्तयः in 8.13

Translation

At this juncture, O king, for the destruction of the enemies of the gods (and) for the well-being of the lion-like immortals, endowed with surpassing valour and strength —

In readable EnglishAt that very moment, O king, to destroy the enemies of the gods and for the good of the foremost of the immortals, (powers) endowed with surpassing valour and might —

The noun the adjective qualifies (‘powers’) is supplied from 8.13.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • एतस्मिन्नन्तरे etasminnantare → एतस्मिन् + अन्तरे

    consonant sandhi (doubling of n) · ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम् (8.3.32)

    A final n after a short vowel is doubled before a vowel: -इन् + अ- → -इन्न-.

  • भवायामरसिंहानामतिवीर्य bhavāyāmarasiṃhānāmativīrya → भवाय + अमरसिंहानाम् + अतिवीर्य-

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); final m before a vowel · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā (भवायामर-); the m of अमरसिंहानाम् is kept before the vowel.

  • बलान्विताः balānvitāḥ → बल + अन्विताः

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सुरद्विषाम् suradviṣām

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    सुर + द्विष्
    Vigraha
    सुरान् द्विषन्ति इति सुरद्विषः, तेषाम् surān dviṣanti iti suradviṣaḥ, teṣām
    Literally
    “of those who hate the gods”
    In the verse
    of the asuras

  • अमरसिंहानाम् amarasiṃhānām

    karmadhāraya (upamita: the standard of comparison stands second, as in पुरुषव्याघ्र)

    Members
    अमर + सिंह
    Vigraha
    अमराः सिंहाः इव, तेषाम् amarāḥ siṃhāḥ iva, teṣām
    Literally
    “of the immortals who are like lions”
    In the verse
    of the foremost gods
    Alternative
    Or ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, अमराणां सिंहाः ‘the lions among the immortals’, i.e. the chief gods. Both mean the leading gods, whose powers come forth in 8.13.

  • अतिवीर्यबलान्विताः ativīryabalānvitāḥ

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa whose first member is a dvandva qualified by the prefix अति

    Members
    अति + वीर्य + बल + अन्वित
    Vigraha
    अतिशयितं वीर्यं च बलं च अतिवीर्यबले; ताभ्याम् अन्विताः atiśayitaṃ vīryaṃ ca balaṃ ca ativīryabale; tābhyām anvitāḥ
    Literally
    “joined with exceeding valour and strength”
    In the verse
    of surpassing might
Morphology & anvaya

  • सुरद्विषाम् suradviṣām

    द्विष् is a root used as a noun at the end of a compound (kvip): consonant stem, genitive plural -आम्.

  • अतिवीर्यबलान्विताः ativīryabalānvitāḥ

    Feminine plural without its noun: it anticipates शक्तयः in the next verse. The sentence runs from 8.12 into 8.13.

Anvaya · prose order

भूप, एतस्मिन् अन्तरे सुरद्विषां विनाशाय अमरसिंहानां भवाय (च) अतिवीर्यबलान्विताः (शक्तयः …)

bhūpa, etasmin antare suradviṣāṃ vināśāya amarasiṃhānāṃ bhavāya (ca) ativīryabalānvitāḥ (śaktayaḥ …)

Time, vocative, two datives of purpose, and an adjective waiting for its noun (शक्तयः) and verb (ययुः) in 8.13.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The purpose is stated before the actors appear: the śaktis come to destroy the asuras and to secure the gods. The vocative भूप reminds us that Medhas is telling this to Suratha.

Traditional interpretation

The paired datives — destruction of the hostile, welfare of the good — follow the familiar formula of divine descent, as in the Bhagavadgītā (4.8: परित्राणाय साधूनां विनाशाय च दुष्कृताम्).

Verse 8.13

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

ब्रह्मेशगुहविष्णूनां तथेन्द्रस्य च शक्तयः।शरीरेभ्यो विनिष्क्रम्य तद्रूपैश्चण्डिकां ययुः॥⁠१३⁠॥

brahmeśaguhaviṣṇūnāṃ tathendrasya ca śaktayaḥ |śarīrebhyo viniṣkramya tadrūpaiścaṇḍikāṃ yayuḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

ब्रह्मेशगुहविष्णूनाम् · तथा · इन्द्रस्य · च · शक्तयःशरीरेभ्यः · विनिष्क्रम्य · तद्रूपैः · चण्डिकाम् · ययुः

brahmeśaguhaviṣṇūnām · tathā · indrasya · ca · śaktayaḥśarīrebhyaḥ · viniṣkramya · tadrūpaiḥ · caṇḍikām · yayuḥ

Word by word

  1. ब्रह्मेशगुहविष्णूनाम्brahmeśaguhaviṣṇūnāmof Brahmā, Īśa (Śiva), Guha (Skanda) and Viṣṇum. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on शक्तयः
  2. तथाtathālikewiseindeclinable (adverb)
  3. इन्द्रस्यindrasyaof Indram. gen. sg. of इन्द्र; depends on शक्तयः
  4. चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
  5. शक्तयःśaktayaḥthe Śaktis, the powersf. nom. pl. of शक्ति (i-stem); subject of ययुः
  6. शरीरेभ्यःśarīrebhyaḥfrom (their) bodiesn. abl. pl. of शरीर; ablative of separation with विनिष्क्रम्य
  7. विनिष्क्रम्यviniṣkramyahaving come forthabsolutive (lyap) of वि + निस् + √क्रम् (bhvādi 1P ‘to step’)
  8. तद्रूपैःtadrūpaiḥwith the forms of those (gods)n. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument of manner
  9. चण्डिकाम्caṇḍikāmto Caṇḍikāf. acc. sg. of चण्डिका; goal of ययुः
  10. ययुःyayuḥwent√या (adādi 2P ‘to go’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada

Translation

— the Śaktis of Brahmā, Īśa, Guha and Viṣṇu, and likewise of Indra, having come forth from their bodies, went to Caṇḍikā with their forms.

In readable English— the Śaktis of Brahmā, Śiva, Skanda and Viṣṇu, and of Indra too, came forth from their bodies and went to Caṇḍikā, each in the form of her god.

Īśa and Guha are rendered by their familiar names, Śiva and Skanda; ‘each in the form of her god’ interprets तद्रूपैः in the light of 8.14.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ब्रह्मेश brahmeśa → ब्रह्म + ईश

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    a + ī → e. The stem ब्रह्मन् drops its n at the head of a compound.

  • तथेन्द्रस्य tathendrasya → तथा + इन्द्रस्य

    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    ā + i → e.

  • शरीरेभ्यो विनिष्क्रम्य śarīrebhyo viniṣkramya → शरीरेभ्यः + विनिष्क्रम्य

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced v becomes -o.

  • तद्रूपैश्चण्डिकां tadrūpaiścaṇḍikāṃ → तद्रूपैः + चण्डिकाम्

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c becomes ś. Inside the compound, तद् + रूप keeps d before the voiced r (झलां जशोऽन्ते, 8.2.39).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • ब्रह्मेशगुहविष्णूनाम् brahmeśaguhaviṣṇūnām

    itaretara dvandva

    Members
    ब्रह्मन् + ईश + गुह + विष्णु
    Vigraha
    ब्रह्मा च ईशः च गुहः च विष्णुः च, तेषाम् brahmā ca īśaḥ ca guhaḥ ca viṣṇuḥ ca, teṣām
    Literally
    “of Brahmā, Īśa, Guha and Viṣṇu”
    In the verse
    four of the five gods whose powers appear; Indra is added separately

  • तद्रूपैः tadrūpaiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    तद् + रूप
    Vigraha
    तेषां रूपाणि, तैः teṣāṃ rūpāṇi, taiḥ
    Literally
    “with the forms of them”
    In the verse
    each in the likeness of her god (explained in 8.14)
Morphology & anvaya

  • विनिष्क्रम्य viniṣkramya

    Absolutive with two prefixes (वि, निस्): the s of निस् becomes ṣ after i, and -य replaces -त्वा because the verb is prefixed.

  • ययुः yayuḥ

    Perfect third plural of √या: reduplication य-यु with the ending -उः; the root ā is lost before it.

Anvaya · prose order

(अतिवीर्यबलान्विताः) ब्रह्मेशगुहविष्णूनां तथा इन्द्रस्य च शक्तयः शरीरेभ्यः विनिष्क्रम्य तद्रूपैः चण्डिकां ययुः।

(ativīryabalānvitāḥ) brahmeśaguhaviṣṇūnāṃ tathā indrasya ca śaktayaḥ śarīrebhyaḥ viniṣkramya tadrūpaiḥ caṇḍikāṃ yayuḥ |

The subject promised in 8.12 (शक्तयः) with its genitives, an absolutive of motion out of the gods’ bodies, and the main verb ययुः with its goal चण्डिकाम्.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The powers of five gods leave their bodies and join Caṇḍikā. As in chapter 2, where the Goddess took shape from the radiance of all the gods, the divine power leaves the gods and gathers in her.

Traditional interpretation

These are the Mothers (मातृगण, 8.39): Brahmāṇī, Māheśvarī, Kaumārī, Vaiṣṇavī, Vārāhī, Nārasiṃhī and Aindrī, introduced one by one in 8.15–21. Vārāhī and Nārasiṃhī are Viṣṇu’s further forms; the gods are five but the Mothers seven.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

In Śrīvidyā worship eight Mothers — Brāhmī, Māheśvarī, Kaumārī, Vaiṣṇavī, Vārāhī, Māhendrī, Cāmuṇḍā and Mahālakṣmī — are worshipped in the outer square (bhūpura) of the Śrīcakra. The list overlaps with this chapter’s Mothers but belongs to a separate liturgical tradition.

Verse 8.14

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

यस्य देवस्य यद्रूपं यथाभूषणवाहनम्।तद्वदेव हि तच्छक्तिरसुरान् योद्धुमाययौ॥⁠१४⁠॥

yasya devasya yadrūpaṃ yathābhūṣaṇavāhanam |tadvadeva hi tacchaktirasurān yoddhumāyayau ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

यस्य · देवस्य · यत् · रूपम् · यथा · भूषणवाहनम्तद्वत् · एव · हि · तच्छक्तिः · असुरान् · योद्धुम् · आययौ

yasya · devasya · yat · rūpam · yathā · bhūṣaṇavāhanamtadvat · eva · hi · tacchaktiḥ · asurān · yoddhum · āyayau

Word by word

  1. यस्यyasyaof whicheverrelative pronoun यद्, m. gen. sg.; qualifies देवस्य
  2. देवस्यdevasyagodm. gen. sg. of देव
  3. यत्yatwhateverrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies रूपम्
  4. रूपम्rūpamformn. nom. sg. of रूप; subject of the relative clause (supply आसीत्)
  5. यथाyathāas, in whatever wayindeclinable (relative adverb of manner)
  6. भूषणवाहनम्bhūṣaṇavāhanamornaments and vehiclen. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  7. तद्वत्tadvatlike that, in the same wayindeclinable (तद् + vati, ‘like that’); correlative of यत् and यथा
  8. एवevaexactly, justindeclinable (restrictive particle)
  9. हिhiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
  10. तच्छक्तिःtacchaktiḥhis Śaktif. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of आययौ
  11. असुरान्asurānthe asurasm. acc. pl. of असुर; object of योद्धुम्
  12. योद्धुम्yoddhumto fightinfinitive (tumun) of √युध् (divādi 4Ā ‘to fight’)
  13. आययौāyayaucameआ + √या (adādi 2P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

Translation

Of whichever god whatever was the form, and as (were his) ornaments and vehicle, just so indeed his Śakti came to fight the asuras.

In readable EnglishWhatever form each god had, whatever his ornaments and his mount, in just that form his Śakti came to fight the asuras.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • यद्रूपं yadrūpaṃ → यत् + रूपम्

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before the voiced r; the m of रूपम् becomes anusvāra before य (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).

  • तद्वदेव tadvadeva → तद्वत् + एव

    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Final t becomes d before a vowel.

  • तच्छक्तिरसुरान् tacchaktirasurān → तच्छक्तिः + असुरान्

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    -iḥ before a vowel → -ir. Inside the compound, तद् + शक्ति → तच्छक्ति: t becomes c next to ś (स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः, 8.4.40), and ś after the stop becomes ch (शश्छोऽटि, 8.4.63).

  • योद्धुमाययौ yoddhumāyayau → योद्धुम् + आययौ

    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel

    The m is kept and written together with the vowel.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • भूषणवाहनम् bhūṣaṇavāhanam

    samāhāra dvandva

    Members
    भूषण + वाहन
    Vigraha
    भूषणं च वाहनं च, तयोः समाहारः bhūṣaṇaṃ ca vāhanaṃ ca, tayoḥ samāhāraḥ
    Literally
    “ornament-and-vehicle”
    In the verse
    his insignia and his mount
    Alternative
    With the preceding यथा the whole may be read as one avyayībhāva, यथाभूषणवाहनम् ‘according to (his) ornaments and vehicle’; the meaning does not change. यथा is taken as a separate relative adverb because it pairs with तद्वत्.

  • तच्छक्तिः tacchaktiḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    तद् + शक्ति
    Vigraha
    तस्य शक्तिः tasya śaktiḥ
    Literally
    “the power of him”
    In the verse
    that god’s own Śakti
Morphology & anvaya

  • तद्वत् tadvat

    The taddhita -वत् (vati) makes an adverb of likeness: तद्वत् ‘like that’. It answers the relatives यत् and यथा.

  • योद्धुम् yoddhum

    Infinitive of purpose with a verb of motion: ‘came to fight’. √युध् + -तुम्: dh + t → ddh.

  • आययौ āyayau

    Perfect third singular of आ + √या: reduplication य-यौ.

Anvaya · prose order

यस्य देवस्य यत् रूपम्, यथा (च) भूषणवाहनम्, तद्वत् एव हि तच्छक्तिः असुरान् योद्धुम् आययौ।

yasya devasya yat rūpam, yathā (ca) bhūṣaṇavāhanam, tadvat eva hi tacchaktiḥ asurān yoddhum āyayau |

A relative–correlative sentence: यत् … यथा … are answered by तद्वत् एव; the main clause is तच्छक्तिः … आययौ.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The rule for all that follows: each Śakti has her god’s form, insignia and mount. Verses 8.15–21 apply it one by one.

Traditional interpretation

In Śākta thought the god acts only through his Śakti. Here the Śaktis come without their gods and fight in their likeness — the power, not its bearer, takes the field.

Verse 8.15

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

हंसयुक्तविमानाग्रे साक्षसूत्रकमण्डलुः।आयाता ब्रह्मणः शक्तिर्ब्रह्माणी साभिधीयते॥⁠१५⁠॥

haṃsayuktavimānāgre sākṣasūtrakamaṇḍaluḥ |āyātā brahmaṇaḥ śaktirbrahmāṇī sābhidhīyate ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

हंसयुक्तविमानाग्रे · साक्षसूत्रकमण्डलुःआयाता · ब्रह्मणः · शक्तिः · ब्रह्माणी · सा · अभिधीयते

haṃsayuktavimānāgre · sākṣasūtrakamaṇḍaluḥāyātā · brahmaṇaḥ · śaktiḥ · brahmāṇī · sā · abhidhīyate

Word by word

  1. हंसयुक्तविमानाग्रेhaṃsayuktavimānāgreat the front of a celestial car yoked with swansn. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  2. साक्षसूत्रकमण्डलुःsākṣasūtrakamaṇḍaluḥwith rosary and water-potf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies शक्तिः
  3. आयाताāyātācame, arrivedpast participle (kta) of आ + √या, f. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
  4. ब्रह्मणःbrahmaṇaḥof Brahmām. gen. sg. of ब्रह्मन् (n-stem); depends on शक्तिः
  5. शक्तिःśaktiḥthe Śaktif. nom. sg. of शक्ति; subject of आयाता
  6. ब्रह्माणीbrahmāṇīBrahmāṇīf. nom. sg. of ब्रह्माणी; predicate with अभिधीयते
  7. साsāshepronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; subject of अभिधीयते
  8. अभिधीयतेabhidhīyateis calledअभि + √धा (juhotyādi 3 ‘to place’; with अभि ‘to name’), karmaṇi prayoga, laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana

Translation

At the front of a car yoked with swans, with rosary and water-pot, came the Śakti of Brahmā; she is called Brahmāṇī.

In readable EnglishBrahmā’s Śakti came seated in a chariot drawn by swans, bearing a rosary and a water-pot. She is called Brahmāṇī.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • विमानाग्रे vimānāgre → विमान + अग्रे

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā; so too स + अक्ष- → साक्ष-.

  • शक्तिर्ब्रह्माणी śaktirbrahmāṇī → शक्तिः + ब्रह्माणी

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    -iḥ before the voiced b → -ir.

  • साभिधीयते sābhidhīyate → सा + अभिधीयते

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ā + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • हंसयुक्तविमानाग्रे haṃsayuktavimānāgre

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, containing a karmadhāraya and a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (हंसयुक्त)

    Members
    हंसयुक्त + विमान + अग्र
    Vigraha
    हंसैः युक्तं हंसयुक्तम्; हंसयुक्तं च तत् विमानं च हंसयुक्तविमानम्; तस्य अग्रम्, तस्मिन् haṃsaiḥ yuktaṃ haṃsayuktam; haṃsayuktaṃ ca tat vimānaṃ ca haṃsayuktavimānam; tasya agram, tasmin
    Literally
    “at the front of the swan-yoked car”
    In the verse
    riding in Brahmā’s swan-drawn chariot

  • साक्षसूत्रकमण्डलुः sākṣasūtrakamaṇḍaluḥ

    bahuvrīhi (saha-bahuvrīhi) on an itaretara dvandva

    Members
    स + अक्षसूत्र + कमण्डलु
    Vigraha
    अक्षसूत्रेण कमण्डलुना च सह वर्तते या सा (अक्षसूत्रम् = अक्षाणां सूत्रम्) akṣasūtreṇa kamaṇḍalunā ca saha vartate yā sā (akṣasūtram = akṣāṇāṃ sūtram)
    Literally
    “she who is with rosary and water-pot”
    In the verse
    bearing Brahmā’s rosary and ascetic’s water-pot
Morphology & anvaya

  • ब्रह्माणी brahmāṇī

    Feminine of ब्रह्मन् with the augment -आन- and -ई (as इन्द्राणी from इन्द्र, रुद्राणी from रुद्र): ‘the consort or power of Brahmā’.

  • अभिधीयते abhidhīyate

    Passive present of अभि + √धा: the root vowel becomes ī before the passive -य- (धीयते). ‘Is named’, with the name in the nominative.

  • साक्षसूत्रकमण्डलुः sākṣasūtrakamaṇḍaluḥ

    A bahuvrīhi ending in a u-stem keeps that stem in the feminine: nominative -उः, not -ऊ.

Anvaya · prose order

हंसयुक्तविमानाग्रे साक्षसूत्रकमण्डलुः ब्रह्मणः शक्तिः आयाता; सा ब्रह्माणी (इति) अभिधीयते।

haṃsayuktavimānāgre sākṣasūtrakamaṇḍaluḥ brahmaṇaḥ śaktiḥ āyātā; sā brahmāṇī (iti) abhidhīyate |

Two clauses: the arrival (शक्तिः आयाता, with place and attributes), then the name (सा ब्रह्माणी अभिधीयते).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The first of the Mothers mirrors Brahmā: the swan-drawn car, the rosary and the water-pot. Her water-pot becomes a weapon when the battle begins (8.33).

Traditional interpretation

The gods praise her in this form in chapter 11 (11.13: हंसयुक्तविमानस्थे ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणि), and at 10.6 the Mothers, ‘with Brahmāṇī at their head’ (ब्रह्माणीप्रमुखाः), return into the one Goddess.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

The Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) names the Goddess ब्राह्मी (name 675), the other form of the same feminine of Brahmā. The two texts use different forms; the identity is of idea, not of wording.

Verse 8.16

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

माहेश्वरी वृषारूढा त्रिशूलवरधारिणी।महाहिवलया प्राप्ता चन्द्ररेखाविभूषणा॥⁠१६⁠॥

māheśvarī vṛṣārūḍhā triśūlavaradhāriṇī |mahāhivalayā prāptā candrarekhāvibhūṣaṇā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

माहेश्वरी · वृषारूढा · त्रिशूलवरधारिणीमहाहिवलया · प्राप्ता · चन्द्ररेखाविभूषणा

māheśvarī · vṛṣārūḍhā · triśūlavaradhāriṇīmahāhivalayā · prāptā · candrarekhāvibhūṣaṇā

Word by word

  1. माहेश्वरीmāheśvarīMāheśvarī, the Śakti of Maheśvara (Śiva)f. nom. sg. of माहेश्वरी (taddhita: महेश्वर + vṛddhi + ṅīp, ‘belonging to Maheśvara’); subject of प्राप्ता
  2. वृषारूढाvṛṣārūḍhāmounted on the bullf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies माहेश्वरी
  3. त्रिशूलवरधारिणीtriśūlavaradhāriṇībearing a mighty tridentf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies माहेश्वरी
  4. महाहिवलयाmahāhivalayāwith great serpents for braceletsf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies माहेश्वरी
  5. प्राप्ताprāptāarrivedpast participle (kta) of प्र + √आप् (svādi 5P ‘to reach’), f. nom. sg.; used as the finite verb
  6. चन्द्ररेखाविभूषणाcandrarekhāvibhūṣaṇāadorned with the crescent moonf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies माहेश्वरी

Translation

Māheśvarī, mounted on the bull, holding an excellent trident, with great serpents as bracelets, adorned with the crescent of the moon, arrived.

In readable EnglishMāheśvarī came, riding the bull and bearing a mighty trident, with great serpents for her bracelets and the crescent moon for her ornament.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • वृषारूढा vṛṣārūḍhā → वृष + आरूढा

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • महाहिवलया mahāhivalayā → महा + अहि + वलया

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    महत् takes the form महा at the head of a karmadhāraya; ā + a → ā. The verse has no external sandhi: every word ends in a vowel before a consonant.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • वृषारूढा vṛṣārūḍhā

    dvitīyā tatpuruṣa

    Members
    वृष + आरूढ
    Vigraha
    वृषम् आरूढा vṛṣam ārūḍhā
    Literally
    “having mounted the bull”
    In the verse
    riding Śiva’s bull

  • त्रिशूलवरधारिणी triśūlavaradhāriṇī

    upapada tatpuruṣa (धारिन्, ‘holding’, as final member), containing a karmadhāraya (त्रिशूलवर, वर ‘excellent’ standing second) and a bahuvrīhi (त्रिशूल)

    Members
    त्रिशूलवर + धारिन्
    Vigraha
    त्रिशूलवरं धारयति इति (त्रिशूलवरम् = वरं त्रिशूलम्; त्रिशूलम् = त्रीणि शूलानि यस्मिन् तत्) triśūlavaraṃ dhārayati iti (triśūlavaram = varaṃ triśūlam; triśūlam = trīṇi śūlāni yasmin tat)
    Literally
    “she who holds an excellent trident”
    In the verse
    bearing Śiva’s trident

  • महाहिवलया mahāhivalayā

    bahuvrīhi containing a karmadhāraya (महाहि)

    Members
    महाहि + वलय
    Vigraha
    महान्तः अहयः वलयानि यस्याः सा (महाहिः = महान् चासौ अहिः च) mahāntaḥ ahayaḥ valayāni yasyāḥ sā (mahāhiḥ = mahān cāsau ahiḥ ca)
    Literally
    “she whose bracelets are great serpents”
    In the verse
    wearing serpents as Śiva does

  • चन्द्ररेखाविभूषणा candrarekhāvibhūṣaṇā

    bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    चन्द्ररेखा + विभूषण
    Vigraha
    चन्द्ररेखा विभूषणं यस्याः सा (चन्द्ररेखा = चन्द्रस्य रेखा) candrarekhā vibhūṣaṇaṃ yasyāḥ sā (candrarekhā = candrasya rekhā)
    Literally
    “she whose ornament is the line of the moon”
    In the verse
    crowned with the crescent
Morphology & anvaya

  • माहेश्वरी māheśvarī

    Vṛddhi of the first syllable (म → मा) forms the adjective ‘of Maheśvara’; the feminine ई makes it the name of his Śakti. Vaiṣṇavī, Kaumārī and Nārasiṃhī are formed the same way.

  • प्राप्ता prāptā

    A past participle standing for a finite verb (‘arrived’), the usual way these verses mark each arrival (8.20, 8.21).

Anvaya · prose order

वृषारूढा त्रिशूलवरधारिणी महाहिवलया चन्द्ररेखाविभूषणा माहेश्वरी प्राप्ता।

vṛṣārūḍhā triśūlavaradhāriṇī mahāhivalayā candrarekhāvibhūṣaṇā māheśvarī prāptā |

One subject (माहेश्वरी), four qualifying compounds, one verb (प्राप्ता).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Śiva’s Śakti carries every mark of Śiva: the bull, the trident, the serpents and the crescent moon.

Traditional interpretation

The gods praise her in the same form at 11.14 (त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे महावृषभवाहिनि).

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

The Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name माहेश्वरी (name 208). There it names the supreme Goddess herself, not one Mother among seven.

Verse 8.17

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›
