Translation

And he too, the Daitya, the great asura Raktabīja, possessed by rage, struck all the Mothers, each separately, with his mace.

In readable EnglishRaktabīja, the great asura, in a fury, struck back at every one of the Mothers in turn with his mace.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • स चापि sa cāpi → सः + च + अपि

    visarga sandhi (special rule for सः); vowel sandhi · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    सः drops its visarga before the consonant c; च + अपि → चापि.

  • सर्वा एवाहनत् sarvā evāhanat → सर्वाः + एव + अहनत्

    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    -āḥ before a vowel loses its visarga and the vowels stand apart (सर्वा एव); एव + अहनत् → एवाहनत्.

  • कोपसमाविष्टो रक्तबीजो महासुरः kopasamāviṣṭo raktabījo mahāsuraḥ → कोपसमाविष्टः + रक्तबीजः + महासुरः

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced r and m becomes -o, twice.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • कोपसमाविष्टः kopasamāviṣṭaḥ

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa

    Members
    कोप + समाविष्ट
    Vigraha
    कोपेन समाविष्टः kopena samāviṣṭaḥ
    Literally
    “entered by anger”
    In the verse
    seized with fury
Morphology & anvaya

  • अहनत् ahanat

    The classical imperfect of √हन् is अहन्. अहनत्, inflected like a thematic (a-stem) verb, is an epic (ārṣa) form; the metre needs its extra syllable (सर्वा एवाहनत् पृथक् is eight syllables, with अहन् it would be seven).

  • मातॄः mātṝḥ

    Accusative plural of the feminine ṛ-stem मातृ: मातॄः, with long ṝ (the masculine would be पितॄन्).

Anvaya · prose order

सः रक्तबीजः महासुरः दैत्यः च अपि कोपसमाविष्टः गदया सर्वाः एव मातॄः पृथक् अहनत्।

saḥ raktabījaḥ mahāsuraḥ daityaḥ ca api kopasamāviṣṭaḥ gadayā sarvāḥ eva mātṝḥ pṛthak ahanat |

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The asura answers each attacker with the mace he carried from the start (8.42 गदापाणिः). पृथक् ‘separately’ shows him striking back at each of the Mothers in turn.

Verse 8.51

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

तस्याहतस्य बहुधा शक्तिशूलादिभिर्भुवि।पपात यो वै रक्तौघस्तेनासञ्छतशोऽसुराः॥⁠५१⁠॥

tasyāhatasya bahudhā śaktiśūlādibhirbhuvi |papāta yo vai raktaughastenāsañchataśo'surāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

तस्य · आहतस्य · बहुधा · शक्तिशूलादिभिः · भुविपपात · यः · वै · रक्तौघः · तेन · आसन् · शतशः · असुराः

tasya · āhatasya · bahudhā · śaktiśūlādibhiḥ · bhuvipapāta · yaḥ · vai · raktaughaḥ · tena · āsan · śataśaḥ · asurāḥ

Word by word

  1. तस्यtasyaof himpronoun तद्, m. gen. sg.
  2. आहतस्यāhatasyastruckआ + √हन् + क्त, m. gen. sg.; qualifies तस्य
  3. बहुधाbahudhāin many ways, many timesindeclinable (taddhita -धा)
  4. शक्तिशूलादिभिःśaktiśūlādibhiḥby spears, tridents and the restm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); instrument with आहतस्य
  5. भुविbhuvion the groundf. loc. sg. of भू
  6. पपातpapātafell√पत् (bhvādi, 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. यःyaḥwhichrelative pronoun यद्, m. nom. sg.; agrees with रक्तौघः
  8. वैvaiindeedindeclinable, emphatic particle
  9. रक्तौघःraktaughaḥflood of bloodm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of पपात
  10. तेनtenafrom it, by itpronoun तद्, m. instr. sg.; correlative of यः
  11. आसन्āsancame into being√अस् (adādi, 2P ‘to be’), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  12. शतशःśataśaḥby hundredsindeclinable (taddhita -शस्)
  13. असुराःasurāḥasurasm. nom. pl. of असुर; subject of आसन्

Translation

The flood of blood which fell indeed on the ground from him, struck many times by spears, tridents and the rest — from it asuras came into being by hundreds.

In readable EnglishStruck again and again by spears, tridents and the rest, he poured a flood of blood upon the ground, and from it asuras arose in their hundreds.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तस्याहतस्य tasyāhatasya → तस्य + आहतस्य

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + ā → ā.

  • रक्तौघस्तेनासञ्छतशोऽसुराः raktaughastenāsañchataśo'surāḥ → रक्तौघः + तेन + आसन् + शतशः + असुराः

    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi; consonant sandhi (n + ś); pūrvarūpa · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40), शश्छोऽटि (8.4.63); अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    रक्तौघः before t → रक्तौघस्; तेन + आसन् → तेनासन्; the final n of आसन् before ś becomes the palatal ñ, and the ś after it is sounded ch (a t is inserted before ś and शश्छोऽटि applies: आसञ्छतशः); शतशः before a becomes शतशो and the a is elided (शतशोऽसुराः).

  • रक्तौघ raktaugha → रक्त + ओघ

    vowel sandhi inside a compound (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)

    a + o → au.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • शक्तिशूलादिभिः śaktiśūlādibhiḥ

    bahuvrīhi containing an itaretara dvandva (शक्तिशूल)

    Members
    शक्ति + शूल + आदि
    Vigraha
    शक्तिश्च शूलं च शक्तिशूले; शक्तिशूले आदी येषां तैः śaktiśca śūlaṃ ca śaktiśūle; śaktiśūle ādī yeṣāṃ taiḥ
    Literally
    “by those of which spear and trident are the first”
    In the verse
    by the spears, tridents and other weapons of the Mothers

  • रक्तौघः raktaughaḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    रक्त + ओघ
    Vigraha
    रक्तस्य ओघः raktasya oghaḥ
    Literally
    “a flood of blood”
    In the verse
    the stream that poured from his wounds
Morphology & anvaya

  • आसन् āsan

    Imperfect third plural of √अस् ‘be’ (आ-सन्), here ‘came to be, arose’.

  • बहुधा bahudhā

    The taddhita -धा forms adverbs of manner from numerals and quantities: बहुधा ‘in many ways’.

Anvaya · prose order

बहुधा शक्तिशूलादिभिः आहतस्य तस्य यः वै रक्तौघः भुवि पपात, तेन शतशः असुराः आसन्।

bahudhā śaktiśūlādibhiḥ āhatasya tasya yaḥ vai raktaughaḥ bhuvi papāta, tena śataśaḥ asurāḥ āsan |

A relative clause (यः … रक्तौघः पपात) and its correlative main clause (तेन … असुराः आसन्).

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The narrator presses the point once more: the Mothers’ weapons cannot end him, because every wound feeds the army they are fighting.

Verse 8.52

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

तैश्चासुरासृक्सम्भूतैरसुरैः सकलं जगत्।व्याप्तमासीत्ततो देवा भयमाजग्मुरुत्तमम्॥⁠५२⁠॥

taiścāsurāsṛksambhūtairasuraiḥ sakalaṃ jagat |vyāptamāsīttato devā bhayamājagmuruttamam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

तैः · च · असुरासृक्सम्भूतैः · असुरैः · सकलम् · जगत्व्याप्तम् · आसीत् · ततः · देवाः · भयम् · आजग्मुः · उत्तमम्

taiḥ · ca · asurāsṛksambhūtaiḥ · asuraiḥ · sakalam · jagatvyāptam · āsīt · tataḥ · devāḥ · bhayam · ājagmuḥ · uttamam

Word by word

  1. तैःtaiḥby thosepronoun तद्, m. instr. pl.; qualifies असुरैः
  2. चcaandindeclinable, conjunctive particle
  3. असुरासृक्सम्भूतैःasurāsṛksambhūtaiḥsprung from the asura’s bloodm. instr. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies असुरैः
  4. असुरैःasuraiḥby asurasm. instr. pl. of असुर; agent of व्याप्तम्
  5. सकलम्sakalamthe wholen. nom. sg. of सकल; qualifies जगत्
  6. जगत्jagatworldn. nom. sg.; subject
  7. व्याप्तम्vyāptamfilled, pervadedवि + √आप् + क्त, n. nom. sg.; predicate with आसीत्
  8. आसीत्āsītwas√अस् (adādi, 2P), laṅ (imperfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable
  10. देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of आजग्मुः
  11. भयम्bhayamfearn. acc. sg. of भय; object of आजग्मुः
  12. आजग्मुःājagmuḥcame to, fell intoआ + √गम् (bhvādi, 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  13. उत्तमम्uttamamutmostn. acc. sg. of उत्तम; qualifies भयम्

Translation

And by those asuras sprung from the asura’s blood the whole world was filled; then the gods fell into the utmost fear.

In readable EnglishThe whole world was overrun by those asuras born of the asura’s blood, and the gods were seized with the greatest fear.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तैश्चासुरासृक्सम्भूतैरसुरैः taiścāsurāsṛksambhūtairasuraiḥ → तैः + च + असुरासृक्सम्भूतैः + असुरैः

    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    तैः before c → तैश्; च + असुर… → चासुर…; …सम्भूतैः before a vowel is sounded r.

  • व्याप्तमासीत्ततो vyāptamāsīttato → व्याप्तम् + आसीत् + ततः

    final m before a vowel; consonant sandhi · खरि च (8.4.55)

    The m stays before the vowel; the t of आसीत् stays unvoiced before the t of ततः and is written joined.

  • भयमाजग्मुरुत्तमम् bhayamājagmuruttamam → भयम् + आजग्मुः + उत्तमम्

    final m before a vowel; visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After u the visarga is sounded r before the vowel of उत्तमम्.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरासृक्सम्भूतैः asurāsṛksambhūtaiḥ

    pañcamī tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (असुरासृक्)

    Members
    असुर + असृज् + सम्भूत
    Vigraha
    असुरस्य असृक् असुरासृक्; असुरासृजः सम्भूताः, तैः asurasya asṛk asurāsṛk; asurāsṛjaḥ sambhūtāḥ, taiḥ
    Literally
    “arisen from the asura’s blood”
    In the verse
    the doubles born of Raktabīja’s blood
Morphology & anvaya

  • असुरासृक्सम्भूतैः asurāsṛksambhūtaiḥ

    असृज् ‘blood’ (n.) appears in a compound with its final j turned to k before the s of सम्भूत. The same noun closes the chapter (8.63 असृङ्मद-).

  • आजग्मुः ājagmuḥ

    Perfect of √गम् with आ: reduplicated ज-ग्म्- (the root vowel drops before the ending -उः). भयम् आजग्मुः ‘they came into fear’.

Anvaya · prose order

तैः च असुरासृक्सम्भूतैः असुरैः सकलं जगत् व्याप्तम् आसीत्; ततः देवाः उत्तमं भयम् आजग्मुः।

taiḥ ca asurāsṛksambhūtaiḥ asuraiḥ sakalaṃ jagat vyāptam āsīt; tataḥ devāḥ uttamaṃ bhayam ājagmuḥ |

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The crisis of the chapter. For the first time in the battle the gods, who have been watching, are afraid: the Mothers’ victories have become a defeat. The turn comes in the next verse, with Caṇḍikā’s command.

Verse 8.53

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

तान् विषण्णान् सुरान् दृष्ट्वा चण्डिका प्राहसत्वरा।उवाच कालीं चामुण्डे विस्तीर्णं वदनं कुरु॥⁠५३⁠॥

tān viṣaṇṇān surān dṛṣṭvā caṇḍikā prāhasatvarā |uvāca kālīṃ cāmuṇḍe vistīrṇaṃ vadanaṃ kuru ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

तान् · विषण्णान् · सुरान् · दृष्ट्वा · चण्डिका · प्राह · सत्वराउवाच · कालीम् · चामुण्डे · विस्तीर्णम् · वदनम् · कुरु

tān · viṣaṇṇān · surān · dṛṣṭvā · caṇḍikā · prāha · satvarāuvāca · kālīm · cāmuṇḍe · vistīrṇam · vadanam · kuru

Word by word

  1. तान्tānthosepronoun तद्, m. acc. pl.; qualifies सुरान्
  2. विषण्णान्viṣaṇṇāndejected, despairingवि + √सद् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to sink’) + क्त, m. acc. pl.; qualifies सुरान्
  3. सुरान्surānthe godsm. acc. pl. of सुर; object of दृष्ट्वा
  4. दृष्ट्वाdṛṣṭvāhaving seen√दृश् + क्त्वा (absolutive)
  5. चण्डिकाcaṇḍikāCaṇḍikāf. nom. sg. of चण्डिका; subject
  6. प्राहprāhaspokeप्र + √अह् (defective ‘to say’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. सत्वराsatvarāin haste, quicklyf. nom. sg. of सत्वर; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies चण्डिका
  8. उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. कालीम्kālīmto Kālīf. acc. sg. of काली; the person addressed (verbs of speaking take the hearer in the accusative)
  10. चामुण्डेcāmuṇḍeO Cāmuṇḍāf. voc. sg. of चामुण्डा
  11. विस्तीर्णम्vistīrṇamwideवि + √स्तॄ (kryādi, 9U ‘to spread’) + क्त, n. acc. sg.; predicate accusative with कुरु
  12. वदनम्vadanammouthn. acc. sg. of वदन; object of कुरु
  13. कुरुkurumake√कृ (tanādi, 8U), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

Translation

Seeing those gods dejected, Caṇḍikā spoke in haste; she said to Kālī: ‘Cāmuṇḍā, make your mouth wide.’

In readable EnglishSeeing the gods in despair, Caṇḍikā spoke at once. She said to Kālī: ‘Cāmuṇḍā, open your mouth wide.’

Textual verification / uncertain: प्राहसत्वरा is divided प्राह सत्वरा ‘spoke in haste’. The string could also be divided प्राहसत् ‘laughed aloud’ + त्वरा, but that would need the spelling प्राहसत्त्वरा and leaves त्वरा (nominative) without a construction; the reading itself is not in doubt.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • कालीं चामुण्डे kālīṃ cāmuṇḍe → कालीम् + चामुण्डे

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra.

  • विस्तीर्णं वदनं कुरु vistīrṇaṃ vadanaṃ kuru → विस्तीर्णम् + वदनम् + कुरु

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    The same change twice.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • सत्वरा satvarā

    bahuvrīhi (सह-bahuvrīhi, सह replaced by स)

    Members
    स + त्वरा
    Vigraha
    त्वरया सह वर्तमाना tvarayā saha vartamānā
    Literally
    “being with haste”
    In the verse
    hurrying, at once
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्राह prāha

    आह is a perfect form of the defective root √अह् ‘to say’, used with present or past sense; with प्र, प्राह ‘spoke out’. Here it announces the speech, and उवाच introduces it — a Purāṇic doubling of verbs of speaking.

  • चामुण्डे cāmuṇḍe

    Vocative of चामुण्डा, the name Kālī received for bringing Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa to the Goddess (7.27). The vocative of an ā-stem feminine ends in -e.

  • विस्तीर्णम् vistīrṇam

    Past participle of वि + √स्तॄ (with -īrṇa): ‘spread out’. विस्तीर्णं वदनं कुरु, with a predicate accusative: ‘make (your) mouth wide’.

Anvaya · prose order

तान् सुरान् विषण्णान् दृष्ट्वा सत्वरा चण्डिका प्राह; (सा) कालीम् उवाच — चामुण्डे, वदनं विस्तीर्णं कुरु।

tān surān viṣaṇṇān dṛṣṭvā satvarā caṇḍikā prāha; (sā) kālīm uvāca — cāmuṇḍe, vadanaṃ vistīrṇaṃ kuru |

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Caṇḍikā answers the gods’ fear with a plan, and the plan is Kālī’s mouth. She addresses Kālī by the name she gave her in 7.27, Cāmuṇḍā.

Traditional interpretation

The hymn of chapter 11 salutes this form as Cāmuṇḍā of the terrible tusked mouth (11.21 दंष्ट्राकरालवदने … चामुण्डे मुण्डमथने). The wide mouth commanded here is the image the hymn remembers.

Verse 8.54

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

मच्छस्त्रपातसम्भूतान् रक्तबिन्दून्महासुरान्।रक्तबिन्दोः प्रतीच्छ त्वं वक्त्रेणानेन वेगिना॥⁠५४⁠॥

macchastrapātasambhūtān raktabindūnmahāsurān |raktabindoḥ pratīccha tvaṃ vaktreṇānena veginā ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

मच्छस्त्रपातसम्भूतान् · रक्तबिन्दून् · महासुरान्रक्तबिन्दोः · प्रतीच्छ · त्वम् · वक्त्रेण · अनेन · वेगिना

macchastrapātasambhūtān · raktabindūn · mahāsurānraktabindoḥ · pratīccha · tvam · vaktreṇa · anena · veginā

Word by word

  1. मच्छस्त्रपातसम्भूतान्macchastrapātasambhūtānarising from the fall of my weaponsm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रक्तबिन्दून्
  2. रक्तबिन्दून्raktabindūnthe drops of bloodm. acc. pl.; ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (as in 8.41); object of प्रतीच्छ
  3. महासुरान्mahāsurānthe great asurasm. acc. pl.; karmadhāraya; a second object of प्रतीच्छ
  4. रक्तबिन्दोःraktabindoḥfrom the drop of bloodm. abl. sg. of रक्तबिन्दु (u-stem); source of महासुरान्
  5. प्रतीच्छpratīcchacatch, receiveप्रति + √इष् (tudādi, 6P ‘to wish’; with प्रति ‘to accept’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  6. त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.
  7. वक्त्रेणvaktreṇawith the mouthn. instr. sg. of वक्त्र; instrument
  8. अनेनanenathispronoun इदम्, n. instr. sg.; qualifies वक्त्रेण
  9. वेगिनाvegināswiftn. instr. sg. of वेगिन्; qualifies वक्त्रेण

Translation

With this swift mouth of yours, catch the drops of blood that arise from the fall of my weapons, and the great asuras (that arise) from a drop of blood.

In readable EnglishWith this swift mouth of yours catch the drops of blood my weapons draw from him, and the great asuras born from those drops.

‘Those drops’ generalises the singular रक्तबिन्दोः ‘from a drop’.

Textual verification / uncertain: रक्तबिन्दोः (singular) is taken as ablative with महासुरान् (‘born from a drop of blood’). It could also be read as a genitive, ‘of Raktabindu’, as another name for Raktabīja; no other verse of the text uses that name, so the ablative is preferred.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • मच्छस्त्र macchastra → मद् + शस्त्र

    consonant sandhi inside a compound (d + ś) · खरि च (8.4.55); स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); शश्छोऽटि (8.4.63)

    The stem मद् ‘my’ ends in t before the unvoiced ś, the t becomes the palatal c, and the ś after it becomes ch: मच्छस्त्र.

  • वक्त्रेणानेन vaktreṇānena → वक्त्रेण + अनेन

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

  • प्रतीच्छ pratīccha → प्रति + इच्छ

    vowel sandhi (prefix + verb, savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    i + i → ī.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • मच्छस्त्रपातसम्भूतान् macchastrapātasambhūtān

    pañcamī tatpuruṣa containing ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣas (मच्छस्त्र, मच्छस्त्रपात)

    Members
    मद् + शस्त्र + पात + सम्भूत
    Vigraha
    मम शस्त्राणि मच्छस्त्राणि; तेषां पातः मच्छस्त्रपातः; तस्मात् सम्भूताः, तान् mama śastrāṇi macchastrāṇi; teṣāṃ pātaḥ macchastrapātaḥ; tasmāt sambhūtāḥ, tān
    Literally
    “arisen from the fall of my weapons”
    In the verse
    the blood my blows will draw from him
Morphology & anvaya

  • प्रतीच्छ pratīccha

    Imperative of प्रति + √इष् (present stem इच्छ-): ‘accept, take in’. The same verb, with सम्, describes Cāmuṇḍā doing it in 8.59 (सम्प्रतीच्छति).

  • रक्तबिन्दोः raktabindoḥ

    Ablative (= genitive) singular of the u-stem रक्तबिन्दु: ‘(born) from the blood-drop’, qualifying महासुरान्. See Uncertain.

Anvaya · prose order

त्वम् अनेन वेगिना वक्त्रेण मच्छस्त्रपातसम्भूतान् रक्तबिन्दून् रक्तबिन्दोः (सम्भूतान्) महासुरान् (च) प्रतीच्छ।

tvam anena veginā vaktreṇa macchastrapātasambhūtān raktabindūn raktabindoḥ (sambhūtān) mahāsurān (ca) pratīccha |

Two objects of प्रतीच्छ: the blood-drops drawn by her weapons, and the asuras born of a drop.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Caṇḍikā’s plan has two parts: Kālī is to catch the blood before it reaches the ground (8.41 made the earth the condition), and to swallow any asura already born of it.

Verse 8.55

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

भक्षयन्ती चर रणे तदुत्पन्नान्महासुरान्।एवमेष क्षयं दैत्यः क्षीणरक्तो गमिष्यति॥⁠५५⁠॥

bhakṣayantī cara raṇe tadutpannānmahāsurān |evameṣa kṣayaṃ daityaḥ kṣīṇarakto gamiṣyati ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

भक्षयन्ती · चर · रणे · तदुत्पन्नान् · महासुरान्एवम् · एषः · क्षयम् · दैत्यः · क्षीणरक्तः · गमिष्यति

bhakṣayantī · cara · raṇe · tadutpannān · mahāsurānevam · eṣaḥ · kṣayam · daityaḥ · kṣīṇaraktaḥ · gamiṣyati

Word by word

  1. भक्षयन्तीbhakṣayantīdevouring√भक्ष् (curādi, 10) + शतृ (present participle), f. nom. sg.; agrees with the subject of चर (you, Kālī)
  2. चरcaramove about, range√चर् (bhvādi, 1P), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  3. रणेraṇein the battlem. loc. sg. of रण
  4. तदुत्पन्नान्tadutpannānborn of itm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies महासुरान्
  5. महासुरान्mahāsurānthe great asurasm. acc. pl.; karmadhāraya; object of भक्षयन्ती
  6. एवम्evamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)
  7. एषःeṣaḥthispronoun एतद्, m. nom. sg.; qualifies दैत्यः
  8. क्षयम्kṣayamto destructionm. acc. sg. of क्षय; goal of गमिष्यति
  9. दैत्यःdaityaḥDaityam. nom. sg.; subject
  10. क्षीणरक्तःkṣīṇaraktaḥdrained of bloodm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies दैत्यः
  11. गमिष्यतिgamiṣyatiwill go√गम् (bhvādi, 1P), lṛṭ (simple future), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

Translation

Range in the battle devouring the great asuras born of it; in this way this Daitya, his blood wasted, will go to destruction.

In readable EnglishRange over the field and devour the great asuras born from his blood. So, drained of blood, this Daitya will perish.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • एवमेष क्षयं evameṣa kṣayaṃ → एवम् + एषः + क्षयम्

    final m before a vowel; visarga sandhi (special rule for एषः) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)

    एवम् stays m before the vowel; एषः drops its visarga before the consonant क्ष.

  • क्षीणरक्तो गमिष्यति kṣīṇarakto gamiṣyati → क्षीणरक्तः + गमिष्यति

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced g becomes -o.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • तदुत्पन्नान् tadutpannān

    pañcamī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    तद् + उत्पन्न
    Vigraha
    तस्मात् उत्पन्नाः, तान् tasmāt utpannāḥ, tān
    Literally
    “arisen from that”
    In the verse
    born from his blood

  • क्षीणरक्तः kṣīṇaraktaḥ

    bahuvrīhi

    Members
    क्षीण + रक्त
    Vigraha
    क्षीणं रक्तं यस्य सः kṣīṇaṃ raktaṃ yasya saḥ
    Literally
    “he whose blood is wasted”
    In the verse
    bled dry
Morphology & anvaya

  • गमिष्यति gamiṣyati

    Simple future of √गम् with connecting i: गम्-इ-ष्यति. क्षयं गम् ‘go to destruction’ is a set phrase for ‘perish’.

  • भक्षयन्ती bhakṣayantī

    Present participle, feminine, agreeing with the unexpressed ‘you’ of चर: ‘range (about), devouring’.

Anvaya · prose order

रणे तदुत्पन्नान् महासुरान् भक्षयन्ती चर; एवम् एषः दैत्यः क्षीणरक्तः क्षयं गमिष्यति।

raṇe tadutpannān mahāsurān bhakṣayantī cara; evam eṣaḥ daityaḥ kṣīṇaraktaḥ kṣayaṃ gamiṣyati |

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The plan’s logic: what cannot be killed by wounding can be killed by draining. Once no blood reaches the ground and none of the doubles survive, the source is exhausted.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa), in a separate work, names the Goddess महाकाली (751), महाग्रासा (752, ‘great devourer’) and महाशना (753, ‘great eater’). The devouring form commanded here is the kind of power such names describe; the two texts remain distinct.

Verse 8.56

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

भक्ष्यमाणास्त्वया चोग्रा न चोत्पत्स्यन्ति चापरे।इत्युक्त्वा तां ततो देवी शूलेनाभिजघान तम्॥⁠५६⁠॥

bhakṣyamāṇāstvayā cogrā na cotpatsyanti cāpare |ityuktvā tāṃ tato devī śūlenābhijaghāna tam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

भक्ष्यमाणाः · त्वया · च · उग्राः · न · च · उत्पत्स्यन्ति · च · अपरेइति · उक्त्वा · ताम् · ततः · देवी · शूलेन · अभिजघान · तम्

bhakṣyamāṇāḥ · tvayā · ca · ugrāḥ · na · ca · utpatsyanti · ca · apareiti · uktvā · tām · tataḥ · devī · śūlena · abhijaghāna · tam

Word by word

  1. भक्ष्यमाणाःbhakṣyamāṇāḥbeing devoured√भक्ष् (curādi) + यक् + शानच् (passive present participle), m. nom. pl.; qualifies उग्राः
  2. त्वयाtvayāby yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent of भक्ष्यमाणाः
  3. चcaandindeclinable, conjunctive particle
  4. उग्राःugrāḥthe fierce onesm. nom. pl. of उग्र, used as a noun (the asuras born of the blood)
  5. नnanotindeclinable, negative particle
  6. चcaandindeclinable, conjunctive particle
  7. उत्पत्स्यन्तिutpatsyantiwill ariseउद् + √पद् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to go’; with उद् ‘to arise’), lṛṭ (simple future), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, in a parasmaipada form
  8. चcaand, alsoindeclinable, conjunctive particle
  9. अपरेapareotherspronominal adjective अपर, m. nom. pl.; subject of उत्पत्स्यन्ति
  10. इतिitithusindeclinable, closing the quoted speech
  11. उक्त्वाuktvāhaving spoken√वच् + क्त्वा (absolutive, with samprasāraṇa: उक्-त्वा)
  12. ताम्tāmto her (Kālī)pronoun तद्, f. acc. sg.; the person addressed
  13. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable
  14. देवीdevīthe Goddess (Caṇḍikā)f. nom. sg. of देवी; subject
  15. शूलेनśūlenawith the spearn. instr. sg. of शूल
  16. अभिजघानabhijaghānastruckअभि + √हन्, liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  17. तम्tamhim (Raktabīja)pronoun तद्, m. acc. sg.; object

Translation

‘And the fierce ones, being devoured by you, (will perish), and others will not arise.’ Having said this to her, the Goddess then struck him with the spear.

In readable English‘As you devour the fierce ones, no others will be born.’ So she spoke to Kālī, and then the Goddess struck him with her spear.

The participial clause is rendered as a temporal clause (‘as you devour …’); the verb understood in the Sanskrit is left implicit.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • भक्ष्यमाणास्त्वया bhakṣyamāṇāstvayā → भक्ष्यमाणाः + त्वया

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s.

  • चोग्रा न चोत्पत्स्यन्ति चापरे cogrā na cotpatsyanti cāpare → च + उग्राः + न + च + उत्पत्स्यन्ति + च + अपरे

    vowel sandhi (guṇa, savarṇa-dīrgha); visarga sandhi · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)

    a + u → o twice (चोग्राः, चोत्पत्स्यन्ति); a + a → ā (चापरे); उग्राः loses its visarga before the voiced n.

  • इत्युक्त्वा ityuktvā → इति + उक्त्वा

    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)

    i becomes y before u.

  • शूलेनाभिजघान śūlenābhijaghāna → शूलेन + अभिजघान

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    a + a → ā.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • भक्ष्यमाणाः bhakṣyamāṇāḥ

    Passive present participle: the passive stem भक्ष्य- (with यक्) + मान: ‘being eaten’.

  • उत्पत्स्यन्ति utpatsyanti

    Future of उद् + √पद् (पद्-स्य → पत्स्य-). The root is ātmanepada and the classical form would be उत्पत्स्यन्ते; the parasmaipada ending is an epic (ārṣa) usage.

  • उक्त्वा uktvā

    Absolutive of √वच् with samprasāraṇa (व → उ): उक्त्वा ‘having said’.

Anvaya · prose order

त्वया च भक्ष्यमाणाः उग्राः (क्षयं यास्यन्ति), अपरे च न उत्पत्स्यन्ति च। इति तां उक्त्वा ततः देवी तं शूलेन अभिजघान।

tvayā ca bhakṣyamāṇāḥ ugrāḥ (kṣayaṃ yāsyanti), apare ca na utpatsyanti ca | iti tāṃ uktvā tataḥ devī taṃ śūlena abhijaghāna |

The first line ends Caṇḍikā’s speech; its first clause leaves the verb to be supplied from 8.55 (‘will perish’). The second line is narrative.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Caṇḍikā ends her instruction and acts at once: now that Kālī is ready to catch the blood, she herself wounds Raktabīja. शूल, translated ‘spear’ here, is also the word for the trident.

Verse 8.57

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

मुखेन काली जगृहे रक्तबीजस्य शोणितम्।ततोऽसावाजघानाथ गदया तत्र चण्डिकाम्॥⁠५७⁠॥

mukhena kālī jagṛhe raktabījasya śoṇitam |tato'sāvājaghānātha gadayā tatra caṇḍikām ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

मुखेन · काली · जगृहे · रक्तबीजस्य · शोणितम्ततः · असौ · आजघान · अथ · गदया · तत्र · चण्डिकाम्

mukhena · kālī · jagṛhe · raktabījasya · śoṇitamtataḥ · asau · ājaghāna · atha · gadayā · tatra · caṇḍikām

Word by word

  1. मुखेनmukhenawith her mouthn. instr. sg. of मुख
  2. कालीkālīKālīf. nom. sg. of काली; subject
  3. जगृहेjagṛhetook in, caught√ग्रह् (kryādi, 9U ‘to seize’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  4. रक्तबीजस्यraktabījasyaRaktabīja’sm. gen. sg.
  5. शोणितम्śoṇitambloodn. acc. sg. of शोणित; object of जगृहे
  6. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable
  7. असौasauhe, that onepronoun अदस्, m. nom. sg.; Raktabīja
  8. आजघानājaghānastruckआ + √हन्, liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  9. अथathathen, in turnindeclinable (particle of sequence)
  10. गदयाgadayāwith his macef. instr. sg. of गदा
  11. तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (adverb of place)
  12. चण्डिकाम्caṇḍikāmCaṇḍikāf. acc. sg.; object of आजघान

Translation

Kālī took Raktabīja’s blood with her mouth. Then he in turn struck Caṇḍikā there with his mace.

In readable EnglishKālī caught Raktabīja’s blood in her mouth. Then he struck at Caṇḍikā there with his mace.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • ततोऽसावाजघानाथ tato'sāvājaghānātha → ततः + असौ + आजघान + अथ

    visarga sandhi; pūrvarūpa; vowel sandhi (ayādi, savarṇa-dīrgha) · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109); एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    ततः before a becomes ततो and the a is elided (ततोऽसौ); the au of असौ becomes āv before the vowel (असावाजघान); आजघान + अथ → आजघानाथ.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • जगृहे jagṛhe

    Perfect of √ग्रह् in the ātmanepada: reduplicated ज-गृह्- with samprasāraṇa of र to ऋ. ‘She took (into her mouth)’.

  • असौ asau

    Nominative masculine singular of the distal pronoun अदस् ‘that (one) yonder’. Before the vowel of आजघान its au becomes āv: असावाजघान.

Anvaya · prose order

काली मुखेन रक्तबीजस्य शोणितं जगृहे; ततः अथ असौ तत्र चण्डिकां गदया आजघान।

kālī mukhena raktabījasya śoṇitaṃ jagṛhe; tataḥ atha asau tatra caṇḍikāṃ gadayā ājaghāna |

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The plan works at once: the blood from Caṇḍikā’s blow goes into Kālī’s mouth, not onto the ground. Raktabīja turns his mace on Caṇḍikā herself.

Modern reflection

A modern reading: Caṇḍikā’s answer to an enemy who multiplies with every wound is not more wounding but removing the source. Kālī does not fight the doubles one by one; she stops them being born.

Verse 8.58

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

न चास्या वेदनां चक्रे गदापातोऽल्पिकामपि।तस्याहतस्य देहात्तु बहु सुस्राव शोणितम्॥⁠५८⁠॥

na cāsyā vedanāṃ cakre gadāpāto'lpikāmapi |tasyāhatasya dehāttu bahu susrāva śoṇitam ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

न · च · अस्याः · वेदनाम् · चक्रे · गदापातः · अल्पिकाम् · अपितस्य · आहतस्य · देहात् · तु · बहु · सुस्राव · शोणितम्

na · ca · asyāḥ · vedanām · cakre · gadāpātaḥ · alpikām · apitasya · āhatasya · dehāt · tu · bahu · susrāva · śoṇitam

Word by word

  1. नnanotindeclinable, negative particle
  2. चcaand, butindeclinable, conjunctive particle
  3. अस्याःasyāḥto her, of herpronoun इदम्, f. gen. sg.; Caṇḍikā
  4. वेदनाम्vedanāmpainf. acc. sg. of वेदना; object of चक्रे
  5. चक्रेcakrecaused, made√कृ (tanādi, 8U), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
  6. गदापातःgadāpātaḥthe blow of the macem. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of चक्रे
  7. अल्पिकाम्alpikāmslightf. acc. sg. of अल्पिका (अल्प + diminutive -क, feminine); qualifies वेदनाम्
  8. अपिapievenindeclinable (particle, here ‘even’ with the negative)
  9. तस्यtasyahispronoun तद्, m. gen. sg.
  10. आहतस्यāhatasyastruckआ + √हन् + क्त, m. gen. sg.; qualifies तस्य
  11. देहात्dehātfrom the bodym. abl. sg. of देह
  12. तुtubutindeclinable, adversative particle
  13. बहुbahumuchn. nom. sg. of बहु; qualifies शोणितम्
  14. सुस्रावsusrāvaflowed√स्रु (bhvādi, 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  15. शोणितम्śoṇitambloodn. nom. sg. of शोणित; subject of सुस्राव

Translation

And the blow of the mace did not cause her even slight pain; but from the body of him, struck, much blood flowed.

In readable EnglishHis mace-blow caused her not the slightest pain, while from his wounded body the blood poured out.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • चास्या वेदनां cāsyā vedanāṃ → च + अस्याः + वेदनाम्

    vowel sandhi; visarga sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)

    च + अस्याः → चास्याः; -āḥ loses its visarga before the voiced v.

  • गदापातोऽल्पिकामपि gadāpāto'lpikāmapi → गदापातः + अल्पिकाम् + अपि

    visarga sandhi with pūrvarūpa; final m before a vowel · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)

    -aḥ before a becomes -o and the a is elided (avagraha); the m of अल्पिकाम् stays before अपि.

  • देहात्तु dehāttu → देहात् + तु

    no change (t before t) · खरि च (8.4.55)

    The final t stays unvoiced before the unvoiced t and is written joined.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • गदापातः gadāpātaḥ

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    गदा + पात
    Vigraha
    गदायाः पातः gadāyāḥ pātaḥ
    Literally
    “the fall of the mace”
    In the verse
    the blow Raktabīja aimed at Caṇḍikā (8.57)
Morphology & anvaya

  • चक्रे cakre

    Perfect ātmanepada of √कृ: reduplicated च-क्र्-, ending -ए. वेदनां चक्रे ‘caused pain’.

  • अल्पिकाम् alpikām

    अल्प ‘small’ with the diminutive -क and feminine -आ (the a before क becomes i): अल्पिका ‘a little’. न … अल्पिकाम् अपि: ‘not even the slightest’.

Anvaya · prose order

गदापातः च अस्याः अल्पिकाम् अपि वेदनां न चक्रे; आहतस्य तस्य देहात् तु बहु शोणितं सुस्राव।

gadāpātaḥ ca asyāḥ alpikām api vedanāṃ na cakre; āhatasya tasya dehāt tu bahu śoṇitaṃ susrāva |

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The contrast is the point: his strongest blow does nothing to Caṇḍikā, while her blow (8.56) makes him bleed. The second line repeats the words of 8.43 (बहु सुस्राव शोणितम्), but this time the blood has somewhere else to go.

Verse 8.59

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

यतस्ततस्तद्वक्त्रेण चामुण्डा सम्प्रतीच्छति।मुखे समुद्गता येऽस्या रक्तपातान्महासुराः॥⁠५९⁠॥

yatastatastadvaktreṇa cāmuṇḍā sampratīcchati |mukhe samudgatā ye'syā raktapātānmahāsurāḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

यतः · ततः · तत् · वक्त्रेण · चामुण्डा · सम्प्रतीच्छतिमुखे · समुद्गताः · ये · अस्याः · रक्तपातात् · महासुराः

yataḥ · tataḥ · tat · vaktreṇa · cāmuṇḍā · sampratīcchatimukhe · samudgatāḥ · ye · asyāḥ · raktapātāt · mahāsurāḥ

Word by word

  1. यतःyataḥfrom whereverindeclinable (relative adverb, -तस्)
  2. ततःtataḥfrom thereindeclinable (correlative adverb, -तस्); यतस्ततः ‘from wherever, from every side’
  3. तत्tatit (the blood)pronoun तद्, n. acc. sg.; refers to शोणितम् of 8.58; object of सम्प्रतीच्छति
  4. वक्त्रेणvaktreṇawith her mouthn. instr. sg. of वक्त्र; instrument
  5. चामुण्डाcāmuṇḍāCāmuṇḍāf. nom. sg.; subject
  6. सम्प्रतीच्छतिsampratīcchaticatches, takes inसम् + प्रति + √इष् (tudādi, 6P), laṭ (present, here historic), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. मुखेmukhein (her) mouthn. loc. sg. of मुख
  8. समुद्गताःsamudgatāḥaroseसम् + उद् + √गम् + क्त, m. nom. pl.; predicate of the relative clause
  9. येyewho, whichrelative pronoun यद्, m. nom. pl.; correlative तान् in 8.60
  10. अस्याःasyāḥherpronoun इदम्, f. gen. sg.; with मुखे
  11. रक्तपातात्raktapātātfrom the falling bloodm. abl. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  12. महासुराःmahāsurāḥgreat asurasm. nom. pl.; karmadhāraya; subject of the relative clause

Translation

From wherever (it flowed), Cāmuṇḍā caught it with her mouth; the great asuras who arose in her mouth from the falling blood —

In readable EnglishWherever it flowed, Cāmuṇḍā caught the blood in her mouth; and the great asuras who sprang up in her mouth from that blood —

The sentence continues into 8.60.

Textual verification / uncertain: तद्वक्त्रेण: taken as two words, तत् (the blood, object of सम्प्रतीच्छति) + वक्त्रेण. A compound तद्वक्त्रेण ‘with that mouth’ is possible; the verb then lacks a stated object, so the two-word reading is preferred.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • यतस्ततस्तद्वक्त्रेण yatastatastadvaktreṇa → यतः + ततः + तत् + वक्त्रेण

    visarga sandhi; consonant sandhi (voicing) · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)

    Visarga before t becomes s, twice; the t of तत् becomes d before the voiced v.

  • येऽस्या रक्तपातान्महासुराः ye'syā raktapātānmahāsurāḥ → ये + अस्याः + रक्तपातात् + महासुराः

    pūrvarūpa; visarga sandhi; consonant sandhi (t before a nasal) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109); भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19); यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)

    ये + अस्याः: the a is elided (येऽस्याः); -āḥ loses its visarga before the voiced r; the t of रक्तपातात् becomes the nasal n before m.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • रक्तपातात् raktapātāt

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    रक्त + पात
    Vigraha
    रक्तस्य पातः, तस्मात् raktasya pātaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally
    “from the fall of blood”
    In the verse
    from the blood as it fell into her mouth
Morphology & anvaya

  • सम्प्रतीच्छति sampratīcchati

    Present of सम् + प्रति + √इष् (stem इच्छ-), used for past narrative (historic present). It fulfils Caṇḍikā’s command प्रतीच्छ (8.54).

  • तत् tat

    Neuter accusative pronoun ‘it’, taking up शोणितम्. It could also be the first member of a compound तद्वक्त्रेण ‘with that mouth’ (8.54 वक्त्रेणानेन); see Uncertain.

Anvaya · prose order

चामुण्डा यतः ततः तत् (शोणितं) वक्त्रेण सम्प्रतीच्छति; रक्तपातात् अस्याः मुखे ये महासुराः समुद्गताः, (तान् …)

cāmuṇḍā yataḥ tataḥ tat (śoṇitaṃ) vaktreṇa sampratīcchati; raktapātāt asyāḥ mukhe ye mahāsurāḥ samudgatāḥ, (tān …)

A main clause, then a relative clause whose correlative (तान्) opens 8.60.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Kālī is now called Cāmuṇḍā by the narrator. She catches every stream of blood (यतस्ततः ‘from wherever’), and even the asuras that still form from it are born inside her mouth.

Verse 8.60

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

तांश्चखादाथ चामुण्डा पपौ तस्य च शोणितम्।देवी शूलेन वज्रेण बाणैरसिभिर्ऋष्टिभिः॥⁠६०⁠॥

tāṃścakhādātha cāmuṇḍā papau tasya ca śoṇitam |devī śūlena vajreṇa bāṇairasibhirṛṣṭibhiḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

तान् · चखाद · अथ · चामुण्डा · पपौ · तस्य · च · शोणितम्देवी · शूलेन · वज्रेण · बाणैः · असिभिः · ऋष्टिभिः

tān · cakhāda · atha · cāmuṇḍā · papau · tasya · ca · śoṇitamdevī · śūlena · vajreṇa · bāṇaiḥ · asibhiḥ · ṛṣṭibhiḥ

Word by word

  1. तान्tānthempronoun तद्, m. acc. pl.; correlative of ये in 8.59; object of चखाद
  2. चखादcakhādadevoured√खाद् (bhvādi, 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  3. अथathathenindeclinable (particle of sequence)
  4. चामुण्डाcāmuṇḍāCāmuṇḍāf. nom. sg.; subject
  5. पपौpapaudrank√पा (bhvādi, 1P ‘to drink’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  6. तस्यtasyahispronoun तद्, m. gen. sg.
  7. चcaandindeclinable, conjunctive particle
  8. शोणितम्śoṇitambloodn. acc. sg.; object of पपौ
  9. देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg.; subject of जघान in 8.61
  10. शूलेनśūlenawith the spearn. instr. sg. of शूल
  11. वज्रेणvajreṇawith the thunderboltm. instr. sg. of वज्र
  12. बाणैःbāṇaiḥwith arrowsm. instr. pl. of बाण
  13. असिभिःasibhiḥwith swordsm. instr. pl. of असि (i-stem)
  14. ऋष्टिभिःṛṣṭibhiḥwith double-edged swordsf. instr. pl. of ऋष्टि (i-stem)

Translation

— them Cāmuṇḍā then devoured, and drank his blood. The Goddess, with spear, thunderbolt, arrows, swords and double-edged swords …

In readable English— Cāmuṇḍā devoured them, and drank his blood. Then the Goddess, with spear and thunderbolt, with arrows, swords and double-edged blades …

The second line is completed by 8.61.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • तांश्चखादाथ tāṃścakhādātha → तान् + चखाद + अथ

    consonant sandhi (n + c); vowel sandhi · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)

    Final n before c becomes anusvāra + ś; चखाद + अथ → चखादाथ (as in 8.38).

  • बाणैरसिभिर्ऋष्टिभिः bāṇairasibhirṛṣṭibhiḥ → बाणैः + असिभिः + ऋष्टिभिः

    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)

    After ai and i the visarga is sounded r before a vowel: बाणैर् before a, असिभिर् before ṛ.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

No compound in this verse needs analysis.

Morphology & anvaya

  • पपौ papau

    Perfect of √पा ‘drink’: reduplicated प-पौ, with the ending -औ that ā-roots take in the first and third singular.

  • देवी devī

    The subject of the next verse’s verb (8.61 जघान). The second line of this verse lists her weapons and leaves the verb for the next.

Anvaya · prose order

अथ चामुण्डा तान् चखाद, तस्य शोणितं च पपौ। देवी शूलेन वज्रेण बाणैः असिभिः ऋष्टिभिः (8.61: तं रक्तबीजं जघान)।

atha cāmuṇḍā tān cakhāda, tasya śoṇitaṃ ca papau | devī śūlena vajreṇa bāṇaiḥ asibhiḥ ṛṣṭibhiḥ (8.61: taṃ raktabījaṃ jaghāna) |

The first line closes the sentence begun in 8.59; the second line opens a new sentence completed in 8.61.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Cāmuṇḍā does both things Caṇḍikā asked: she eats the asuras born of the blood and drinks the blood itself. Meanwhile the Goddess attacks with every weapon at once.

Comparative Śrīvidyā notes

Comparative Śākta / Śrīvidyā

Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the names महाग्रासा (752) and महाशना (753), ‘great devourer’ and ‘great eater’, beside महाकाली (751). They name in general terms the power Cāmuṇḍā shows here; the two texts are separate works.

Verse 8.61

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

जघान रक्तबीजं तं चामुण्डापीतशोणितम्।स पपात महीपृष्ठे शस्त्रसङ्घसमाहतः॥⁠६१⁠॥

jaghāna raktabījaṃ taṃ cāmuṇḍāpītaśoṇitam |sa papāta mahīpṛṣṭhe śastrasaṅghasamāhataḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

जघान · रक्तबीजम् · तम् · चामुण्डापीतशोणितम्सः · पपात · महीपृष्ठे · शस्त्रसङ्घसमाहतः

jaghāna · raktabījam · tam · cāmuṇḍāpītaśoṇitamsaḥ · papāta · mahīpṛṣṭhe · śastrasaṅghasamāhataḥ

Word by word

  1. जघानjaghānastruck down√हन् (adādi, 2P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada; subject देवी (8.60)
  2. रक्तबीजम्raktabījamRaktabījam. acc. sg.; object
  3. तम्tamthatpronoun तद्, m. acc. sg.; qualifies रक्तबीजम्
  4. चामुण्डापीतशोणितम्cāmuṇḍāpītaśoṇitamwhose blood had been drunk by Cāmuṇḍām. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रक्तबीजम्
  5. सःsaḥhepronoun तद्, m. nom. sg.
  6. पपातpapātafell√पत् (bhvādi, 1P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  7. महीपृष्ठेmahīpṛṣṭheon the surface of the earthn. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
  8. शस्त्रसङ्घसमाहतःśastrasaṅghasamāhataḥstruck by a mass of weaponsm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सः

Translation

(The Goddess) struck down that Raktabīja, whose blood had been drunk by Cāmuṇḍā. Struck by the mass of weapons, he fell on the surface of the earth,

In readable Englishstruck down Raktabīja, whose blood Cāmuṇḍā had drunk. Smitten by that host of weapons, he fell to the ground,

The first line completes the sentence begun in 8.60; the second runs on into 8.62.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • रक्तबीजं तं चामुण्डा raktabījaṃ taṃ cāmuṇḍā → रक्तबीजम् + तम् + चामुण्डा…

    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)

    Final m before a consonant is written as anusvāra, twice.

  • स पपात sa papāta → सः + पपात

    visarga sandhi (special rule for सः) · एतत्तदोः सुलोपोऽकोरनञ्समासे हलि (6.1.132)

    सः drops its visarga before a consonant.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • चामुण्डापीतशोणितम् cāmuṇḍāpītaśoṇitam

    bahuvrīhi containing a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (चामुण्डापीत)

    Members
    चामुण्डा + पीत + शोणित
    Vigraha
    चामुण्डया पीतं चामुण्डापीतम्; चामुण्डापीतं शोणितं यस्य सः, तम् cāmuṇḍayā pītaṃ cāmuṇḍāpītam; cāmuṇḍāpītaṃ śoṇitaṃ yasya saḥ, tam
    Literally
    “him whose blood was drunk by Cāmuṇḍā”
    In the verse
    Raktabīja, bled dry

  • महीपृष्ठे mahīpṛṣṭhe

    ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa

    Members
    मही + पृष्ठ
    Vigraha
    मह्याः पृष्ठम्, तस्मिन् mahyāḥ pṛṣṭham, tasmin
    Literally
    “on the back of the earth”
    In the verse
    on the ground

  • शस्त्रसङ्घसमाहतः śastrasaṅghasamāhataḥ

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (शस्त्रसङ्घ)

    Members
    शस्त्र + सङ्घ + समाहत
    Vigraha
    शस्त्राणां सङ्घः शस्त्रसङ्घः; तेन समाहतः śastrāṇāṃ saṅghaḥ śastrasaṅghaḥ; tena samāhataḥ
    Literally
    “struck by a host of weapons”
    In the verse
    cut down by all the weapons together
Morphology & anvaya

  • चामुण्डापीतशोणितम् cāmuṇḍāpītaśoṇitam

    पीत is the past participle of √पा ‘drink’. The bahuvrīhi carries the whole explanation of his death in one word: his blood was drunk, so no new asura could rise.

  • पपात papāta

    Perfect of √पत् with the reduplicated syllable प- (contrast the unreduplicated plural पेतुः, 8.35).

Anvaya · prose order

(देवी) चामुण्डापीतशोणितं तं रक्तबीजं जघान; शस्त्रसङ्घसमाहतः सः महीपृष्ठे पपात।

(devī) cāmuṇḍāpītaśoṇitaṃ taṃ raktabījaṃ jaghāna; śastrasaṅghasamāhataḥ saḥ mahīpṛṣṭhe papāta |

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The death of Raktabīja. Each detail matters: the blood had been drunk, so the many weapons that had multiplied him before now kill him.

Verse 8.62

Śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

नीरक्तश्च महीपाल रक्तबीजो महासुरः।ततस्ते हर्षमतुलमवापुस्त्रिदशा नृप॥⁠६२⁠॥

nīraktaśca mahīpāla raktabījo mahāsuraḥ |tataste harṣamatulamavāpustridaśā nṛpa ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

नीरक्तः · च · महीपाल · रक्तबीजः · महासुरःततः · ते · हर्षम् · अतुलम् · अवापुः · त्रिदशाः · नृप

nīraktaḥ · ca · mahīpāla · raktabījaḥ · mahāsuraḥtataḥ · te · harṣam · atulam · avāpuḥ · tridaśāḥ · nṛpa

Word by word

  1. नीरक्तःnīraktaḥbloodlessm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate with पपात of 8.61
  2. चcaandindeclinable, conjunctive particle
  3. महीपालmahīpālaO protector of the earth (king)m. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); Medhas addresses Suratha
  4. रक्तबीजःraktabījaḥRaktabījam. nom. sg.; in apposition to सः of 8.61
  5. महासुरःmahāsuraḥthe great asuram. nom. sg.; karmadhāraya
  6. ततःtataḥthenindeclinable
  7. तेtetheypronoun तद्, m. nom. pl.; qualifies त्रिदशाः
  8. हर्षम्harṣamjoym. acc. sg. of हर्ष; object of अवापुः
  9. अतुलम्atulamincomparablem. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies हर्षम्
  10. अवापुःavāpuḥattainedअव + √आप् (svādi, 5P ‘to obtain’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
  11. त्रिदशाःtridaśāḥthe godsm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject
  12. नृपnṛpaO kingm. voc. sg. of नृप

Translation

— and bloodless, O protector of the earth, the great asura Raktabīja. Then those gods attained incomparable joy, O king.

In readable Englishdrained of his blood, O lord of the earth — Raktabīja, the great asura. Then the gods, O king, were filled with boundless joy.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • नीरक्तश्च nīraktaśca → नीरक्तः + च

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)

    Visarga before c becomes ś.

  • नीरक्त nīrakta → निर् + रक्त

    consonant sandhi inside a compound (r before r) · रो रि (8.3.14), ढ्रलोपे पूर्वस्य दीर्घोऽणः (6.3.111)

    The r of निर् is dropped before the r of रक्त, and the vowel before it is lengthened: नि → नी.

  • ततस्ते tataste → ततः + ते

    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)

    Visarga before t becomes s; likewise अवापुस्त्रिदशाः.

  • हर्षमतुलमवापुस्त्रिदशा नृप harṣamatulamavāpustridaśā nṛpa → हर्षम् + अतुलम् + अवापुः + त्रिदशाः + नृप

    final m before a vowel; visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34); भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)

    Two final m’s stay before vowels; अवापुः before t → अवापुस्; त्रिदशाः loses its visarga before the voiced n.

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • नीरक्तः nīraktaḥ

    bahuvrīhi (prādi-bahuvrīhi with निर्)

    Members
    निर् + रक्त
    Vigraha
    निर्गतं रक्तं यस्मात् सः nirgataṃ raktaṃ yasmāt saḥ
    Literally
    “he from whom the blood has gone out”
    In the verse
    drained of all his blood

  • महीपाल mahīpāla

    upapada tatpuruṣa

    Members
    मही + पाल
    Vigraha
    महीं पालयति इति महीपालः mahīṃ pālayati iti mahīpālaḥ
    Literally
    “protector of the earth”
    In the verse
    king — Suratha, the listener

  • अतुलम् atulam

    bahuvrīhi (nañ-bahuvrīhi)

    Members
    अ + तुला
    Vigraha
    न विद्यते तुला यस्य सः, तम् na vidyate tulā yasya saḥ, tam
    Literally
    “that which has no equal-weight”
    In the verse
    beyond compare

  • त्रिदशाः tridaśāḥ

    bahuvrīhi (numerical)

    Members
    त्रि + दश
    Vigraha
    त्रिः (त्रिगुणाः) दश परिमाणम् एषां ते triḥ (triguṇāḥ) daśa parimāṇam eṣāṃ te
    Literally
    “those whose number is thrice ten”
    In the verse
    the gods, by the round number for the thirty-three
    Alternative
    A traditional etymology reads तिस्रः दशाः (अवस्थाः) येषां ते, ‘those who have (only) three stages of life’ (without old age). The numerical reading is the usual one.
Morphology & anvaya

  • अवापुः avāpuḥ

    Perfect of अव + √आप्: the perfect of √आप् is आप- (आपुः ‘they obtained’); with अव, अवापुः.

  • महीपाल mahīpāla

    Vocative: Medhas, the narrator, turns to King Suratha. नृप at the end of the line is a second vocative to the same listener.

Anvaya · prose order

महीपाल, (सः) रक्तबीजः महासुरः नीरक्तः च (पपात)। नृप, ततः ते त्रिदशाः अतुलं हर्षम् अवापुः।

mahīpāla, (saḥ) raktabījaḥ mahāsuraḥ nīraktaḥ ca (papāta) | nṛpa, tataḥ te tridaśāḥ atulaṃ harṣam avāpuḥ |

The first line completes 8.61 (सः पपात); the second is a new sentence.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

Medhas, the sage telling the story, addresses King Suratha twice (महीपाल, नृप), marking the turn of the episode. The gods, afraid in 8.52, now rejoice.

Verse 8.63

Half-śloka · anuṣṭubh
‹›

तेषां मातृगणो जातो ननर्तासृङ्मदोद्धतः॥⁠६३⁠॥ॐ

teṣāṃ mātṛgaṇo jāto nanartāsṛṅmadoddhataḥ ||

Padaccheda · the words separated

तेषाम् · मातृगणः · जातः · ननर्त · असृङ्मदोद्धतः

teṣām · mātṛgaṇaḥ · jātaḥ · nanarta · asṛṅmadoddhataḥ

Word by word

  1. तेषाम्teṣāmfrom them, of them (the gods)pronoun तद्, m. gen. pl.; with जातः (‘born of them’)
  2. मातृगणःmātṛgaṇaḥthe band of Mothersm. nom. sg.; ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (as in 8.39); subject of ननर्त
  3. जातःjātaḥborn√जन् + क्त, m. nom. sg.; qualifies मातृगणः
  4. ननर्तnanartadanced√नृत् (divādi, 4P ‘to dance’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
  5. असृङ्मदोद्धतःasṛṅmadoddhataḥwild with the intoxication of bloodm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies मातृगणः

Translation

The band of Mothers born of them danced, wild with the intoxication of blood.

In readable EnglishAnd the band of Mothers born from the gods danced, wild with the blood they had drunk.

‘From the gods’ makes तेषाम् explicit; ‘they had drunk’ interprets असृङ्मद ‘intoxication of blood’.

Textual verification / uncertain: तेषाम् could also be read with the whole clause (‘their band of Mothers’, i.e. the gods’ own), or as ‘among them (the gods)’. Taking it with जातः, ‘born of them’, agrees with 8.13.

Sandhi & sandhi-viccheda

  • मातृगणो जातो mātṛgaṇo jāto → मातृगणः + जातः

    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    -aḥ before the voiced j becomes -o; जातः before the n of ननर्त likewise becomes जातो.

  • ननर्तासृङ्मदोद्धतः nanartāsṛṅmadoddhataḥ → ननर्त + असृक् + मद + उद्धतः

    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha); inside the compound, nasal assimilation and guṇa · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)

    ननर्त + असृक्… : a + a → ā. Inside the compound the k of असृक् becomes the nasal ṅ before m (असृङ्मद), and मद + उद्धत gives a + u → o (मदोद्धत).

Samāsa & samāsa-vigraha

  • असृङ्मदोद्धतः asṛṅmadoddhataḥ

    tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (असृङ्मद)

    Members
    असृज् + मद + उद्धत
    Vigraha
    असृजः मदः असृङ्मदः; तेन उद्धतः asṛjaḥ madaḥ asṛṅmadaḥ; tena uddhataḥ
    Literally
    “exalted by the intoxication of blood”
    In the verse
    frenzied with the blood they had drunk
Morphology & anvaya

  • ननर्त nanarta

    Perfect of √नृत्: reduplicated न-नर्त, with guṇa of ṛ to ar in the singular.

  • असृङ्मदोद्धतः asṛṅmadoddhataḥ

    उद्धत is the past participle of उद् + √हन् (‘raised up’, hence ‘wild, unrestrained’). असृज् ‘blood’ is the same noun as in 8.52.

Anvaya · prose order

तेषां (देवानां) जातः मातृगणः असृङ्मदोद्धतः ननर्त।

teṣāṃ (devānāṃ) jātaḥ mātṛgaṇaḥ asṛṅmadoddhataḥ nanarta |

A single clause; the genitive तेषाम् is taken with जातः.

Śākta commentary

Direct textual meaning

The chapter ends with the Mothers’ victory dance. तेषाम् recalls their origin: they came out of the bodies of the gods (8.13). This last mantra is a half-śloka; in recitation it carries the closing bīja ॐ, which belongs to the pāṭha, not to the verse.

Traditional interpretation

The Mothers return to fight beside the Goddess in the next chapter (9.8 कृत्वा युद्धं तु मातृभिः) and are praised one by one in the hymn of chapter 11 (11.13–20).

❁

इति श्रीमार्कण्डेयपुराणे सावर्णिके मन्वन्तरे देवीमाहात्म्ये रक्तबीजवधो नामाष्टमोऽध्यायः॥

iti śrīmārkaṇḍeyapurāṇe sāvarṇike manvantare devīmāhātmye raktabījavadho nāmāṣṭamo'dhyāyaḥ ||

Reflecting on the chapter

Śumbha, having lost his generals, sends the whole Daitya host and goes with it. The Goddess answers first with sound, then with company: the powers of Brahmā, Śiva, Skanda, Viṣṇu and Indra come to her in their gods’ forms, and her own Śakti sends Śiva to the asuras with the gods’ terms. The Mothers rout the host, but Raktabīja, whose blood becomes asuras wherever it falls, turns every blow into more enemies until the gods despair. Caṇḍikā’s answer is Cāmuṇḍā’s open mouth: the blood is drunk, the doubles are eaten, and Raktabīja falls, drained. The chapter ends with the Mothers’ dance (8.63); the brothers themselves are still to come (chapter 9).

Verses for study and memorisation

  • 8.13The Śaktis of Brahmā, Śiva, Skanda, Viṣṇu and Indra leave their bodies and go to Caṇḍikā.
  • 8.14Whatever form, ornaments and mount each god had, in just that form his Śakti came to fight.
  • 8.28Because she made Śiva himself her messenger, she became known in the world as Śivadūtī.
  • 8.41Whenever a drop of Raktabīja’s blood falls to the ground, an asura of his stature springs up from the earth.
  • 8.53Seeing the gods in despair, Caṇḍikā tells Kālī: ‘Cāmuṇḍā, open your mouth wide.’
  • 8.61The Goddess strikes down Raktabīja, whose blood Cāmuṇḍā has drunk, and he falls to the ground.
  • 8.63The band of Mothers born from the gods dances, wild with blood: the chapter’s last half-verse.
Vocabulary, constructions and insights

Principal vocabulary

  • शक्तिःśaktiḥpower; a god’s power in female form (8.13), and also the spear (8.17, 8.34)
  • मातृगणःmātṛgaṇaḥthe band of Mothers: the gods’ Śaktis as one host (8.39, 8.63)
  • मातृmātṛmother; the Mothers (मातृभिः 8.45, मातॄः 8.50)
  • रक्तबीजःraktabījaḥRaktabīja, ‘he whose blood is seed’
  • रक्तबिन्दुःraktabinduḥa drop of blood
  • शोणितम्śoṇitamblood (8.43, 8.57, 8.61)
  • असृज्asṛjblood, in compounds असृक्- and असृङ्- (8.52, 8.63)
  • शिवदूतीśivadūtīŚivadūtī, ‘she whose messenger is Śiva’ (8.28)
  • दौत्यम्dautyamthe office of messenger (8.28)
  • चामुण्डाcāmuṇḍāCāmuṇḍā, Kālī’s name since 7.27
  • वाहनम्vāhanama god’s mount (8.14)
  • लीलाlīlāplay, ease: लीलया ‘as if in play’ (8.31)
  • अट्टहासःaṭṭahāsaḥa loud, terrible laugh (8.38)
  • हविर्भुज्havirbhuj‘eater of the oblation’: the gods (8.26)

Important grammatical constructions

  • The perfect as the narrative past, including the unreduplicated पेतुः, नेशुः (8.35, 8.39) and the periphrastic पूरयामास, ताडयामास (8.8, 8.47).
  • Third-person imperatives for commands to the absent (निर्यान्तु, 8.4; लभताम्, सन्तु, 8.26) and a passive imperative (हन्यन्ताम्, 8.22).
  • Relative–correlative pairs that carry the logic of the chapter: यस्य … यत् … तद्वत् (8.14), यदा … तदा (8.41), यावन्तः … तावन्तः (8.44), यतः … ततः (8.28).
  • The present for a standing fact (पतति, 8.41) and for past narrative (धावति स्म, 8.33; सम्प्रतीच्छति, 8.59).
  • One singular verb for several subjects, each with her own instrument (जघान, 8.34, 8.49).
  • Vṛddhi taddhitas forming the Mothers’ names (माहेश्वरी, वैष्णवी, ऐन्द्री, नारसिंही), with ब्रह्माणी in -ānī.
  • Epic (ārṣa) forms kept as printed: युयुधुः (8.45), अहनत् (8.50), उत्पत्स्यन्ति (8.56), जिग्ये (8.10).
  • Hiatus at the pāda break, printed so in the Gita Press: निर्यान्तु | आज्ञया (8.6), ताडयामास | ऐन्द्री (8.47).

Śākta insights

  • The gods’ powers are feminine and can act without their gods: the Śaktis leave the gods’ bodies and fight in their likeness while the gods look on (8.13–14, 8.22).
  • Each Mother is the one Mother in a particular form: Kaumārī is called अम्बिका (8.17), and the Mothers are named as one band (8.39, 8.63); at 10.5–6 the Goddess claims them as her own and takes them back into herself.
  • The Goddess sends the highest god as her envoy (8.24), and the name she earns, Śivadūtī, records it (8.28).
  • Her terms are restoration, not destruction: Indra’s rule, the gods’ offerings, and life for the asuras in Pātāla (8.26).
  • Some enemies grow with every blow: the Mothers’ weapons multiply Raktabīja (8.43–52), and he dies only when his blood is received before it reaches the ground (8.53–61).

Śrīvidyā connections

The one specific connection is liturgical, not in the Purāṇa text: the Gita Press dhyāna before this chapter is, with one change of wording (बाणचापहस्ताम् for पुष्पबाणचापाम्), the second dhyāna verse of the Lalitā Sahasranāma — red Bhavānī with noose, goad, bow and arrow, surrounded by the powers beginning with aṇimā, contemplated as ‘I’. The Lalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) names the same four weapons (8, रागस्वरूपपाशाढ्या; 9, क्रोधाकाराङ्कुशोज्ज्वला; 10, मनोरूपेक्षुकोदण्डा; 11, पञ्चतन्मात्रसायका) and the name Bhavānī (112, भवानी). In Śrīvidyā worship eight Mothers (Brāhmī, Māheśvarī, Kaumārī, Vaiṣṇavī, Vārāhī, Māhendrī, Cāmuṇḍā, Mahālakṣmī) are honoured in the outer square of the Śrīcakra; the list overlaps with this chapter’s Mothers but belongs to a separate liturgical tradition. Several names of the chapter are also names in the Lalitā Sahasranāma: Caṇḍikā (755, चण्डिका), Ambikā (295, अम्बिका), Kātyāyanī (556, कात्यायनी), Śivadūtī (405, शिवदूती), Māheśvarī (208, माहेश्वरी) and Vaiṣṇavī (892, वैष्णवी), there names of the supreme Goddess, and Brāhmī (675, ब्राह्मी), a different form from Brahmāṇī; Vārāhī appears there in another battle (76, विशुक्रप्राणहरणवाराहीवीर्यनन्दिता), and Mahākālī, Mahāgrāsā and Mahāśanā (751–753, महाकाली, महाग्रासा, महाशना) name in general terms the devouring power Cāmuṇḍā shows here. These are shared Śākta names, not evidence that one text depends on the other; the chapter itself states no specifically Śrīvidyā doctrine.

Numbering
Gita Press counts every uvāca line and half-śloka as a mantra; the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa (GRETIL, adhyāya 88) numbers by full ślokas and does not number uvāca lines, so its numbers differ.

Variant readings (kept as printed)
8.41: तत्प्रमाणस्तदासुरः / तत्प्रमाणो महासुरः (Textual Verification Required)
Uncertain readings or interpretations

Sandhi patterns
  • -aḥ before voiced consonants → -o (शुम्भो भैरवशासनः, क्रुद्धो रक्तबीजो महासुरः), and before a → -o with avagraha (गदापातोऽल्पिकाम्, ततोऽसौ, शतशोऽसुराः); pūrvarūpa after -e as well (तेऽपि, येऽस्याः).
  • -āḥ before a voiced sound → -ā (कालका दौर्हृदा मौर्याः, पुरुषा जाताः); visarga → r before vowels and voiced consonants (ऋषिरुवाच, सहस्रैर्बहुभिर्वृतः); visarga → s/ś before t/c (सिंहस्तथा, पुनश्च); सः and एषः lose their visarga (स पपात, एवमेष क्षयम्).
  • Final n before c/t/ś: n → ṃś/ṃs and ñ (विदारितांश्चान्यान्, पतितांस्तान्, बाणाञ्छूल-, आसञ्छतशः); n before j → ñ (दैत्याञ्जघान); t + ś → cch (चाकरोच्छत्रून्, मच्छिवाः, मच्छस्त्र-).
  • t/d voiced before voiced sounds (तद्वदेव, चेद्भवन्तः, शरीराद्रक्त-) and assimilated before n (तन्नादम्, रक्तपातान्महासुराः).
  • Savarṇa-dīrgha, guṇa, vṛddhi and yaṇ across words (ममाज्ञया, चोपबृंहयत्, तथैवैन्द्री, क्षयितेष्वसुरेश्वरः); ayādi in दानवावतिगर्वितौ and असावाजघान.
  • Two declared hiatuses at the pāda break: निर्यान्तु | आज्ञया (8.6) and ताडयामास | ऐन्द्री (8.47).
Samāsa constructions
  • Bahuvrīhis of description throughout the Mothers’ portraits (साक्षसूत्रकमण्डलुः, महाहिवलया, मयूरवरवाहना, वज्रहस्ता, सहस्रनयना, सटाक्षेपक्षिप्तनक्षत्रसंहतिः) and of Raktabīja (रक्तबीजः, तत्प्रमाणः, तद्रूपाः, क्षीणरक्तः, नीरक्तः, चामुण्डापीतशोणितम्).
  • Tṛtīyā tatpuruṣas of means and agent in the battle (शूलपातविदारितान्, खट्वाङ्गपोथितान्, तुण्डप्रहारविध्वस्ताः, शिवदूत्यभिदूषिताः, शस्त्रसङ्घसमाहतः) and pañcamī tatpuruṣas of origin (असुरासृक्सम्भूतैः, मच्छस्त्रपातसम्भूतान्, तदुत्पन्नान्).
  • Ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣas of possession and rule (असुरेश्वरः, तच्छक्तिः, चण्डिकाशक्तिः, रक्तबिन्दुः, ऐन्द्रीकुलिशपातेन) and upapada tatpuruṣas with agent nouns (सुरद्विषाम्, हविर्भुजः, त्रिशूलवरधारिणी, शिवाशतनिनादिनी).
  • Itaretara dvandvas of names and weapons (ब्रह्मेशगुहविष्णूनाम्, शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गखड्ग-, शूलशक्तिपरश्वधान्, दैत्यदानवाः), a samāhāra dvandva (भूषणवाहनम्), numeral compounds (षडशीतिः, चतुरशीतिः) and prādi compounds with अति (अतिभीषणा, अत्युग्रा, अतिकोपना).
Grammatical observations
  • Several sentences run across verses: 8.2–3, 8.12–13, 8.59–60, 8.60–61 and 8.61–62; the anvayas say so.
  • आयान्तम् (8.8) is read strictly as masculine, of Śumbha who set out in 8.7, not loosely of the army.
  • जिग्ये (8.10) is taken as active ‘outdid’, supported by 9.22 and 9.26; परिवारिताः (8.11) is feminine though the lion is among its subjects.
  • Epic forms are kept and explained: augmentless उपबृंहयत् (8.9), parasmaipada युयुधुः (8.45) and उत्पत्स्यन्ति (8.56), thematic अहनत् (8.50).
  • प्राहसत्वरा (8.53) is divided प्राह सत्वरा; रक्तबिन्दोः (8.54) is read as an ablative, not a name; तद्वक्त्रेण (8.59) as two words, तत् + वक्त्रेण.
  • शक्ति keeps both senses in the chapter: the gods’ powers (8.13) and Skanda’s spear (8.17, 8.34).
Philosophical themes
  • The Mothers as the gods’ powers
  • Śivadūtī and the envoy reversed
  • Raktabīja: multiplication by wounding
  • Cāmuṇḍā drinking the blood
Open questions
  • 8.41: तत्प्रमाणस्तदासुरः (Gita Press) / तत्प्रमाणो महासुरः (GRETIL, Freund) — Textual Verification Required; the Gita Press reading is kept, and the sense is the same.
  • 8.47: Aindrī strikes with a mace (गदया), though her weapon elsewhere in the chapter is the thunderbolt; no variant is recorded, and the text is translated as written.
  • Interpretive doubts, none about the reading, are flagged on 8.4 (उदायुधाः as epithet or clan name; the noun counted), 8.8 (आयान्तम् of Śumbha or of the army), 8.10 (the construction of जिग्ये), 8.32 (whose front तस्य अग्रतः means), 8.37 (अन्यान् with विदारितान् or apart), 8.39 (नेशुः ‘fled’ or ‘perished’), 8.53 (the division of प्राहसत्वरा), 8.54 (रक्तबिन्दोः as ablative or a name), 8.59 (तद्वक्त्रेण as one word or two) and 8.63 (the construction of तेषाम्).
